\subsection{Consolidated main theorem block}\label{subsec:main-theorem-boundary}

\begin{paperthm}[Constrained D-MAP record law]\label{thm:main-record-wrapper}
Let \((A_n)_{n\in\mathbb Z}\) have the stationary visible law generated by the
sampled-counter kernels in \Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main}.  Put
\[
p=e^{-\Gamma\Delta\tau},\qquad
s=e^{-\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau},\qquad
\rho=\PP(A_0=1),\qquad
\lambda_{\rm hid}=p-b .
\]
Then \((A_n)\) is a full-support renewal process.  If \(G\) is the Palm number
of zeros between consecutive ones, then
\[
g_k=\PP(G=k)=e_D^\top T_0^kT_1{\bf 1},\qquad k\ge0 .
\]
For \(\Gamma\ne\kappa_{\mathrm r}\),
\[
g_k=\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}(1-p)p^k-\Gamma(1-s)s^k}
          {\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma},
\qquad
S_k=\PP(G\ge k)=
\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}p^k-\Gamma s^k}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma},
\]
with continuous-limit formulas on \(\Gamma=\kappa_{\mathrm r}\).  The zero-run
hazard \(h_k=g_k/S_k\) is strictly increasing.  The visible covariance has the
single hidden-mode tail
\[
\operatorname{Cov}(A_0,A_n)=\rho(a-\rho)\lambda_{\rm hid}^{\,n-1},
\qquad n\ge1 .
\]
  The record is exact \(1\)-dependent if and only if
  \(\lambda_{\rm hid}=0\).  Moreover
  \[
  \gamma_1=\rho(a-\rho)<0,
  \]
  so adjacent outputs are not independent and the record is not
  \(0\)-dependent.  In no positive parameter case is it a finite-order Markov
  chain.
\end{paperthm}

\begin{corollary}[Sharp hidden-mode bound and correlation phase]
\label{cor:sharp-hidden-mode-phase-main}
Put \(x=\Gamma\Delta\tau\), \(y=\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau\), and
\(f(t)=te^{-t}\).  The nontrivial hidden eigenvalue satisfies
\[
\lambda_{\rm hid}=\frac{f(y)-f(x)}{y-x}\quad(x\ne y),\qquad
\lambda_{\rm hid}=f'(x)=e^{-x}(1-x)\quad(x=y),
\]
and its sharp global range is
\[
-e^{-2}\le\lambda_{\rm hid}<1.
\]
Equality in the lower bound holds only at \(x=y=2\), whereas the upper
endpoint is approached only as \(x,y\downarrow0\).  Since
\(\rho(a-\rho)<0\), all positive-lag covariances are negative when
\(\lambda_{\rm hid}>0\); at \(\lambda_{\rm hid}=0\), only the lag-one
covariance is nonzero; and when \(\lambda_{\rm hid}<0\), the covariances
strictly alternate in sign.  In the last case their successive absolute
ratio is universally bounded by
\[
\left|\frac{\operatorname{Cov}(A_0,A_{n+1})}
{\operatorname{Cov}(A_0,A_n)}\right|\le e^{-2},\qquad n\ge1.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The symmetric hidden-mode formula in the proof of Theorem~A gives the stated
secant slope.  For \(x\ne y\), the mean-value theorem writes it as
\(f'(\xi)\) for a point \(\xi\) strictly between \(x\) and \(y\).  Now
\(f'(t)=e^{-t}(1-t)\) has its unique global minimum \(-e^{-2}\) at \(t=2\)
and has supremum \(1\) as \(t\downarrow0\).  A nondegenerate secant cannot
attain the lower endpoint, because that would require \(f'\) to equal its
strict global minimum throughout a nontrivial interval.  Hence equality is
possible only in the diagonal limit \(x=y=2\).  The covariance assertions
then follow directly from Theorem~A.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Small-sampling-interval cycle and intensity bias]
\label{prop:small-sampling-interval-bias-main}
Fix \(\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r}>0\), write the variable sampling interval as
\(\Delta>0\), and let \(\mu_\Delta=\EE(G+1)=1/\rho_\Delta\).  With
\(x=\Gamma\Delta\) and \(y=\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\),
\[
\mu_\Delta=\frac{y/(1-e^{-x})-x/(1-e^{-y})}{y-x},
\]
where the equal-rate case is the continuous limit.  As \(\Delta\downarrow0\),
\[
\Delta\mu_\Delta=\frac1\Gamma+\frac1{\kappa_{\mathrm r}}
+\frac{\Delta}{2}
+\frac{\Gamma\kappa_{\mathrm r}(\Gamma+\kappa_{\mathrm r})}{720}\Delta^4
+O(\Delta^6),
\]
and therefore
\[
\frac{\rho_\Delta}{\Delta}
=\frac{\Gamma\kappa_{\mathrm r}}{\Gamma+\kappa_{\mathrm r}}
-\frac{\Delta}{2}
\left(\frac{\Gamma\kappa_{\mathrm r}}{\Gamma+\kappa_{\mathrm r}}\right)^2
+O(\Delta^2).
\]
Thus boundary sampling adds a universal half-interval term to the mean
physical inter-click time and produces the displayed first-order downward
bias in the click intensity per unit time.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Summing \((k+1)g_k\) in Theorem~A gives
\[
\mu_\Delta=\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}/(1-e^{-\Gamma\Delta})
-\Gamma/(1-e^{-\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta})}
{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma},
\]
which is the displayed dimensionless formula.  Substitution of
\[
\frac{u}{1-e^{-u}}=1+\frac u2+\frac{u^2}{12}
-\frac{u^4}{720}+\frac{u^6}{30240}+O(u^8)
\]
and cancellation of the quadratic terms yield the expansion of
\(\Delta\mu_\Delta\), including its continuous equal-rate limit.  Expanding
its reciprocal gives the intensity formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{paperthm}[Quotient inverse]\label{thm:toeplitz_image_inverse_duality}
Work in the same constrained D-MAP record model.  Let
\[
r_0=\rho,\qquad r_1=\rho a,\qquad r_2=\rho u_2,
\]
and assume the quotient inverse domain
\[
r_0>0,\qquad \Delta_{\rm inv}:=r_1-r_0^2\ne0 .
\]
Define
\[
a(r)=\frac{r_1}{r_0},\qquad
\lambda(r)=\frac{r_2-r_0^2}{r_1-r_0^2},
\]
and
\[
\Phi(r)=
\left(1-a(r)+\lambda(r),\,
r_0(1-\lambda(r))-a(r)+\lambda(r)\right).
\]
On the constrained D-MAP image,
\[
\Phi(r)=(p+s,ps).
\]
Thus the visible law determines the unordered survival-factor pair
\(\{p,s\}\), equivalently the unordered roots of
\[
z^2-\Phi_1(r)z+\Phi_2(r)=0,
\]
and determines all visible renewal probabilities through this quotient.  For
known design interval \(\Delta\tau\), the same quotient determines the
unordered numerical rate set
\[
\left\{-\frac{\log p}{\Delta\tau},-\frac{\log s}{\Delta\tau}\right\}
=\{\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r}\}.
\]
If \(\Delta\tau\) is not supplied as a known design interval, the numerical
rate display is replaced by the dimensionless identifiable set
\[
\{-\log p,-\log s\}
=\{\Gamma\Delta\tau,\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau\}.
\]
In either case the visible law does not determine an ordered labelled vector
\((\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r})\) unless external side information fixes the
physical assignment.  On the exchange diagonal, the
invariant visible-law estimand is the common dimensionless product, and only
for known \(\Delta\tau\) the common numerical rate, not an ordered
attempt/recovery label.
\end{paperthm}

\begin{proposition}[Exact three-inclusion image]

 succeeded in 234ms:
\begin{abstract}
We study a two-state killed-reset discrete-time Markovian arrival process
(D-MAP) through its stationary binary output.  The deterministic post-arrival
reset makes the visible process renewal, but it does not make the hidden
representation identifiable.  We derive the Palm gap law, its increasing
zero-run hazard, the one-mode covariance tail, the exact \(1\)-dependence
criterion, and finite-word probabilities.

Three visible inclusion probabilities give a rational inverse to the symmetric
survival coordinates \((p+s,ps)\).  This three-inclusion formula is presented
as a candidate original result pending complete reduction against canonical
DMAP2 coordinates; it identifies the unordered pair \(\{p,s\}\), not a
labelled realization.  Intersecting the weak-equivalence class with the
killed-reset Markov cone gives an explicit two-state fibre arc whose endpoints
are the two rate-swapped triangular kernels and which collapses on the exchange
diagonal.  For minimal interior \(n\)-state kernels, the fibre contains a local
positivity-preserving similarity manifold of dimension \((n-1)^2\).  The pure
serial result is stated as a finite-coordinate sampled-tail corollary of
generalized-Erlang and Coxian representation theory.

For a fixed model and prespecified complete-cycle convention, we prove a
three-inclusion central limit theorem, a complete-visible-law specification
test, and the quotient delta method.  The sorted-root and log-rate limits are
consequences of that delta method rather than separate contributions.  A
root-free physical-image test is also given under explicit compactness and
uniform-margin hypotheses.  Abstract Markov--Palm tangent calculations, the
moving-dimensional Helmert Gaussian coupling, an exact complete-Palm-gap
singular subexperiment, and a pointwise projection example are collected in
the supplement.  The supplement distinguishes the Bickel--Kwon
known-marginal Markov projection from the additional killed-reset constraints.
\end{abstract}

\noindent\textbf{Keywords:} constrained D-MAP, phase-type identifiability,
weak equivalence, similarity orbit, renewal process, quotient inversion,
specification testing

\section{Introduction}

The applied-probability question is what the binary arrival record of a
constrained D-MAP determines when every marked transition resets the hidden
chain to a fixed state.  This reset creates renewal structure, while the usual
nonuniqueness of Markovian and phase-type representations remains.  The model
therefore sits at the intersection of renewal theory, D-MAP weak equivalence,
phase-type representation, and fixed-setting inference.

Our two-state parametrization has hidden states \(R\) and \(D\).  During a
sampling interval of length \(\Delta\tau\), an attempt clock has rate
\(\Gamma\) and a recovery clock has rate \(\kappa_{\mathrm r}\).  A marked
transition is latched in \(D\) until the next boundary.  This construction is
only a realization of the probability model: the results concern the labelled
kernels and their visible binary law.  Allowing a marked transition into
\(R\) changes the model and removes the deterministic regeneration used below.

Classical MAP and D-MAP theory supplies the arrival-process framework
\cite{Neuts1979MAP,Lucantoni1991BMAP,AlfaNeuts1995DMAP}.  Zamparo's
renewal-binary results supply the finite-dimensional renewal-law language and
the finite-order Markov criterion used here \cite{Zamparo2022RenewalBinary}.
Ram\'irez-Cobo and Lillo give the complete weak-equivalence classification for
MAP2 and MAP3 processes \cite{RamirezCoboLillo2012WeakEquivalentMAP};
M\'esz\'aros and Telek give canonical coordinates for discrete MAP2/RAP2
models \cite{MeszarosTelek2013DiscreteMAP2Canonical}.  Those results are the
primary comparison objects for both the quotient inverse and the killed-reset
cone intersection.  The direct three-inclusion inverse below is a candidate
original result pending complete reduction against canonical DMAP2
coordinates.  It is not asserted as an established priority claim.

For serial kernels, generalized-Erlang/Coxian representation and minimality
theory are the primary antecedents \cite{HeZhang2008MinimalCoxian,
HeZhang2009GeneralizedErlang}.  Our serial statement is limited to the
finite-coordinate sampled-tail specialization, including confluent repeated
poles.  For general killed-reset kernels, realization uniqueness identifies a
similarity orbit, but only its intersection with the Markov cone consists of
valid stochastic representations.  This distinction leads to an explicit
two-state cone-intersection arc and a local dimension statement on the minimal
interior stratum; neither is a claim about the global dimension of every fibre.

The five lettered results have the following fixed roles and dependencies:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{>{\bfseries}c p{0.30\textwidth} p{0.53\textwidth}}
\toprule
Result & Statement & Logical dependence \\
\midrule
A & Killed-reset record law & Assumption~1.1 and deterministic post-click reset. \\
B & Candidate three-inclusion quotient inverse & Theorem~A and the nonzero covariance chart. \\
C & Fixed-setting inclusion CLT & Theorem~A, complete retained cycles, and stopped separated batches. \\
D & Complete-visible-law specification test & Theorem~A for the null word law; its finite-range local conclusion uses the declared Markov-gap path. \\
E & Quotient delta method & Theorems~B and C on a stable separated-root chart. \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The physical-image test, two-state fibre arc, serial corollary, and minimal
interior similarity-manifold result use ordinary theorem numbering.  The
Markov--Palm tangent, moving-dimensional Helmert coupling, exact
complete-Palm-gap split experiment, and pointwise projection calculation are
supplementary results.  In particular, Bickel--Kwon's known-marginal Markov
tangent and additive projection are antecedents of the supplementary tangent
calculation \cite{BickelKwon2001Semiparametric}; the added restrictions there
are the killed-reset atom constraint, positivity-compatible path realization,
and calendar-time scaling.

The specification statistic imports distributional-distance consistency from
stationary-ergodic testing \cite{RyabkoRyabko2010ErgodicInference,
Ryabko2012StationaryTesting}.  The physical-image statistic instead tests the
finite-dimensional inclusion image under the sampled-counter model.  These are
different questions, and neither diagnostic is used to select a model and then
authorize another inferential statement from the same record.

\begin{assumption}[Constrained sampled-counter D-MAP model]\label{ass:model-inference-contract-main}
Fix \((\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r},\Delta\tau)\in(0,\infty)^3\) and define
\[
p=e^{-\Gamma\Delta\tau},\qquad
s=e^{-\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau},
\]
\[
a=\int_0^{\Delta\tau}\kappa_{\mathrm r}e^{-\kappa_{\mathrm r}r}
\bigl(1-e^{-\Gamma(\Delta\tau-r)}\bigr)\,dr,\qquad
b=\int_0^{\Delta\tau}\kappa_{\mathrm r}e^{-\kappa_{\mathrm r}r}
e^{-\Gamma(\Delta\tau-r)}\,dr .
\]
The record model is the two-state D-MAP with hidden states ordered as
\((R,D)\) and labelled kernels
\[
T_0=\begin{pmatrix}p&0\\ b&s\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
T_1=\begin{pmatrix}0&1-p\\0&a\end{pmatrix}.
\]
The convention is
\((T_i)_{XY}=\PP(A_n=i,H_{n+1}=Y\mid H_n=X)\), the visible process
\((A_n)_{n\in\mathbb Z}\) is the stationary binary process generated by these
kernels, and formulas on \(\Gamma=\kappa_{\mathrm r}\) are interpreted by
continuous limits.  The killed entries \(T_1(R,R)=T_1(D,R)=0\) are a structural
constraint of this D-MAP, not optional boundary cases of the finite-record
theorems.
\end{assumption}

\subsection{Scope of finite-record reports}\label{subsec:finite-record-report-scope-main}

The retained-record limits are fixed-setting asymptotic statements inside the
stationary law specified in \Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main}.  The
model, population point, complete-cycle convention, and deterministic batch
sequence are fixed before the record is interpreted.  Theorems~C and D are CLT
and specification-test statements, respectively; Theorem~E is the quotient
delta method.  The pointwise projection example is supplementary and has no
confidence-set, testing, or coverage conclusion.

The only model interpretation assumed by this contract is the latched
killed-leakage sampled-counter protocol of
\Cref{lem:classical-counter-kernel-construction-main}.  If the sampling
interval \(\Delta\tau\) is a known design interval, the visible stationary
record identifies the unordered survival-factor set
\(\{p,s\}=\{e^{-\Gamma\Delta\tau},e^{-\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau}\}\) and
hence the unordered numerical rate set
\(\{\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r}\}\).  It does not identify which member is the
attempt rate and which is the recovery rate without side information.  If
\(\Delta\tau\) is not treated as a known design interval, the visible law
identifies only the dimensionless unordered product set
\(\{\Gamma\Delta\tau,\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau\}\); the same record does
not put those products on an absolute numerical rate scale.

The supplementary singular likelihood calculation observes a deterministic
number of independent complete Palm gaps.  Its exact product experiment is
uniform for \(z\) in compact subsets of \((0,1)\) and bounded local split
\(h\), and it identifies only \(h^2\).  It does not establish an endpoint
factorization or asymptotic equivalence for the stationary finite record.  The
separate projection result is only the pointwise limit at the preselected
population point \(z_0=1/2\), using the printed covariance certificate in
\Cref{lem:z-half-diagonal-branch-certificate-main}.  No confidence set, test,
critical value, coverage conclusion, or uniform projection claim is attached
to it.
Ordered labelled rates require external side information fixed independently
of this record.  The paper does not claim
simultaneous inversion over \(0<z<1\), nor identification of UDW, KMS,
thermodynamic, response-profile, or ordered-rate quantities unless the
particular result supplies the extra hypotheses and proof.

\subsection{Visible-record diagnostics for the model contract}\label{subsec:visible-record-model-diagnostics-main}

\Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main} and
\Cref{subsec:finite-record-report-scope-main} form a conditional contract, not a
model-selection theorem.  The retained-record limits below are statements

 succeeded in 216ms:
\section{Killed-reset fibres: an exact two-state arc and higher-state structure}

The minimal-realization statements in this subsection are recalled background,
not contributions of this paper.  Kalman's similarity theorem is the classical
source, and minimal MAP/phase-type formulations and canonical-coordinate
developments are given in
\cite{Kalman1963LinearSystems,TelekHorvath2007MinimalMAP,
BladtNielsen2017MatrixExponential,MeszarosTelek2013DiscreteMAP2Canonical,
PappTelek2013DiscretePHCanonical}.  We state the consequence needed to locate
the visible fibre, classify the complete Markovian fibre of the physical
two-state kernel, and then prove the higher-state quantitative assertion: at a
minimal interior killed-reset kernel the admissible fibre has local dimension
\((n-1)^2\).  Nonunique MAP and Coxian representatives are documented in
\cite{RamirezCoboLilloWiper2010MAP2Nonidentifiability,
RamirezCoboLillo2012WeakEquivalentMAP,RizkBurkeWalsh2019CoxianNonuniqueness}.
Graph-specific time-to-event inverses are treated algebraically by
Radulescu et al.\ \cite{RadulescuEtAl2025TimeToEventIdentifiability}; the
criterion below instead isolates the exact orbit fibre for any declared
minimal killed-reset subclass.

\paragraph{Recalled minimal-realization uniqueness.}
Let \((a,A,b)\) and \((\widetilde a,\widetilde A,\widetilde b)\) be two real
scalar realizations of the same dimension \(n\) such that
\[
aA^k b=\widetilde a\widetilde A^k\widetilde b
\qquad(k\ge0).
\]
Assume that both reachability matrices
\[
[\,b,Ab,\ldots,A^{n-1}b\,],\qquad
[\,\widetilde b,\widetilde A\widetilde b,\ldots,
\widetilde A^{n-1}\widetilde b\,]
\]
and both observability matrices
\[
\begin{bmatrix}a\\aA\\\vdots\\aA^{n-1}\end{bmatrix},
\qquad
\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde a\\\widetilde a\widetilde A\\\vdots\\
\widetilde a\widetilde A^{n-1}\end{bmatrix}
\]
have rank \(n\). Then there is a unique \(M\in\operatorname{GL}_n(\RR)\)
such that
\[
\widetilde a=aM,
\qquad \widetilde A=M^{-1}AM,
\qquad \widetilde b=M^{-1}b.
\]
These rank hypotheses are precisely properties \textup{(R)} and \textup{(L)}
of Bladt and Nielsen, whose Theorem~4.2.16 characterizes minimality by their
simultaneous validity; the corresponding intertwining relations are given in
their Lemmas~4.2.5--4.2.6
\cite[Theorem~4.2.16, p.~216, and Lemmas~4.2.5--4.2.6,
p.~212]{BladtNielsen2017MatrixExponential}. This standard result is invoked
here and is not claimed as a new theorem; see also Kalman
\cite{Kalman1963LinearSystems}, Telek--Horv\'ath
\cite{TelekHorvath2007MinimalMAP}, and
\cite{Ryden1996Aggregated,OCinneide1989PhaseTypeNonuniqueness,
OCinneide1991InvariantPolytopes}.

\begin{proposition}[Recalled orbit-fibre consequence of minimal realization]
\label{thm:nstate-killed-reset-identifiability-dichotomy-main}
Fix \(n\ge2\), put \(\beta=e_n^\top\), and let
\[
\mathcal K_n=\{K\in\RR^{n\times n}:K\ge0,\ K\mathbf1\le\mathbf1,\
\operatorname{spr}(K)<1\}.
\]
For \(K\in\mathcal K_n\), define the killed-reset D-MAP by
\[
T_0=K,\qquad T_1=(I-K)\mathbf1\beta
\]
and its Palm survival coordinates by \(S_k(K)=\beta K^k\mathbf1\).
Call \(K\) minimal when both
\[
\mathcal C(K)=[\,\mathbf1,K\mathbf1,\ldots,K^{n-1}\mathbf1\,],
\qquad
\mathcal O(K)=
\begin{bmatrix}\beta\\ \beta K\\ \vdots\\ \beta K^{n-1}\end{bmatrix}
\]
have rank \(n\). Let
\[
\mathcal G_n=\{M\in\operatorname{GL}_n(\RR):
M\mathbf1=\mathbf1,\ \beta M=\beta\},
\]
and let \(\mathfrak P_n\subset\mathcal G_n\) be the reset-preserving
permutation matrices.

For any declared subclass \(\mathcal C\subset\mathcal K_n\) of minimal
kernels, fix the structural equivalence \(\sim_{\mathcal C}\) under which
identification is requested, and assume that \(K\sim_{\mathcal C}L\) implies
equality of visible laws. Examples are reset-preserving hidden-state
relabeling and, in a serial rate chart, permutation of the rate coordinates.
Define the Markovian orbit fibre
\[
\mathfrak F_{\mathcal C}(K)=
\left\{M^{-1}KM\in\mathcal C:M\in\mathcal G_n\right\}
\big/\!\sim_{\mathcal C}.
\]
Then standard minimal-realization uniqueness gives the following exact
characterization.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item Two minimal kernels \(K,L\in\mathcal K_n\) generate the same stationary
visible click law if and only if
\[
\begin{aligned}
L&=M^{-1}KM &&\text{for the unique }M\in\mathcal G_n\\
&&&\text{implementing their normalized minimal realizations}.
\end{aligned}
\]
\item Equivalently, the declared hidden object is identifiable from the visible law within
\(\mathcal C\), modulo \(\sim_{\mathcal C}\), if and only if
\[
\#\mathfrak F_{\mathcal C}(K)=1\qquad\text{for every }K\in\mathcal C.
\]
Pointwise identifiability at \(K\) is equivalent to the same condition at
that \(K\); if the cardinality exceeds one, its distinct classes are exactly
the hidden nonidentifiability fibre. Thus this assertion is the orbit-fibre
reformulation of part \textup{(i)}.
\end{enumerate}
Part~\textup{(ii)} is deliberately recorded only as terminology: after the
fibre has been defined, singleton fibre and identifiability modulo
\(\sim_{\mathcal C}\) are the same assertion.  Neither part is claimed as a
new realization theorem.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Deterministic reset makes the visible process renewal. Its Palm gap tail is
exactly \(S_k(K)\), so equality of visible stationary laws is equivalent to
equality of all the scalar coordinates \(\beta K^k\mathbf1\), \(k\ge0\).
Apply the minimal-realization theorem stated above to the triples
\((\beta,K,\mathbf1)\) and \((\beta,L,\mathbf1)\). Its hypotheses are exactly
the two displayed rank conditions in the proposition, and it gives a unique
similarity \(L=M^{-1}KM\). Preservation of the fixed input and output vectors
gives \(M\mathbf1=\mathbf1\) and \(\beta M=\beta\), respectively.
Conversely, these two identities give
\[
\beta(M^{-1}KM)^k\mathbf1=\beta K^k\mathbf1
\]
for every \(k\), proving (i). Taking the intersection with
\(\mathcal C\) and then quotienting precisely the declared structural
equivalence proves
the necessary-and-sufficient assertion (ii).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Complete two-state fibre arc and exchange dichotomy]
\label{thm:two-state-complete-fibre-arc-main}
Under \Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main}, put \(K=T_0\),
\(q_\ast=\Gamma/\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), and
\[
 I_\ast=[\min\{1,q_\ast\},\max\{1,q_\ast\}].
\]
Then \(K\) is minimal, and its complete visible-law fibre in
\(\mathcal K_2\) is the injectively parametrized closed arc
\[
 \{K(q):q\in I_\ast\},
 \qquad
 K(q)=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 p-b+bq & \displaystyle\frac{b(1-q)(q-q_\ast)}q\\[5pt]
 bq & s+b(1-q)
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{F1}
\]
Here complete means that every \(L\in\mathcal K_2\) with the same stationary
visible click law as \(K\) is \(K(q)\) for exactly one \(q\in I_\ast\).
Writing \(T_0(\alpha,\eta)\) for the no-click kernel constructed with attempt
rate \(\alpha\) and recovery rate \(\eta\), the endpoints are the two physical
triangular kernels obtained by exchanging those rates:
\[
 K(1)=T_0(\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r}),
 \qquad
 K(q_\ast)=T_0(\kappa_{\mathrm r},\Gamma).
 \tag{F2}
\]
If \(\Gamma\ne\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), every \(q\) strictly between the
endpoints gives a strictly positive kernel with strictly positive killing
deficits, and the physical triangular subfamily meets the arc only at the two
endpoints.  If \(\Gamma=\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), then \(I_\ast=\{1\}\), so
the entire two-state killed-reset fibre is a singleton.  Thus the unrestricted
two-state hidden kernel is identifiable exactly on the exchange diagonal and
has a continuum fibre off it; within the physical sampled-counter subfamily,
the only ambiguity off the diagonal is the rate exchange.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
First \(b>0\).  If \(\Gamma\ne\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), direct evaluation of
the defining integral gives
\[
 b=\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}(p-s)}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma},
 \qquad
 b+s-p=\frac{\Gamma(p-s)}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma}
          =bq_\ast>0.
 \tag{F3}
\]
The same identities hold by continuity on the diagonal, where
\(p=s\), \(b=\Gamma\Delta\tau p>0\), and \(q_\ast=1\).  Consequently
\[
 \det[\mathbf1,K\mathbf1]=b+s-p=bq_\ast>0,
 \qquad
 \det\begin{bmatrix}e_2^\top\\e_2^\top K\end{bmatrix}=-b\ne0,
\]
so \(K\) is minimal.

Every member of \(\mathcal G_2\) has the unique form
\[
 M_q=\begin{pmatrix}q&1-q\\0&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad q\ne0.
\]
Multiplication, using \(b+s-p=bq_\ast\), gives
\(M_q^{-1}KM_q=K(q)\) in \textup{(F1)}.  Since \(K\) is minimal, its scalar
Hankel sequence has rank two.  Any \(L\in\mathcal K_2\) with the same visible
law has the same leading Hankel matrix; its factorization as
\(\mathcal O(L)\mathcal C(L)\) has rank two, so both factors have rank two and
\(L\) is minimal as well.  The recalled orbit--fibre proposition therefore
forces every such \(L\) to have the displayed form.  If
\(K(q)\in\mathcal K_2\), its lower-left entry
is \(bq\ge0\).  Invertibility excludes \(q=0\), hence \(q>0\).  Its
upper-right entry then yields the exact condition
\[
 \frac{b(1-q)(q-q_\ast)}q\ge0
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad q\in I_\ast.
 \tag{F4}
\]

Conversely, let \(q\in I_\ast\).  The two diagonal entries in
\textup{(F1)} are affine in \(q\), with endpoint values \(p,s\) and \(s,p\),
respectively, and are therefore positive.  The lower-left entry is positive,
and \textup{(F4)} makes the upper-right entry nonnegative.  Writing
\(a=1-b-s>0\), the two killing deficits are
\[
 \mathbf1-K(q)\mathbf1
 =\begin{pmatrix}a+bq_\ast/q\\a\end{pmatrix}>0.
 \tag{F5}
\]
Similarity preserves the spectral radius
\(\max\{p,s\}<1\), so \(K(q)\in\mathcal K_2\).  This proves that the
displayed arc is the complete fibre.  It is injective because its lower-left
entry is \(bq\).

At \(q=1\) one recovers \(K\).  At \(q=q_\ast\),
\[
 K(q_\ast)=\begin{pmatrix}s&0\\bq_\ast&p\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Equation~\textup{(F3)} says that \(bq_\ast\) is precisely the no-click
recovery-to-ready probability after exchanging the two rates, and its second
row has the same killing deficit \(a\); this proves \textup{(F2)}.  If
\(q_\ast\ne1\), the product in the upper-right entry is strictly positive
inside \(I_\ast\) and vanishes only at its endpoints.  All remaining entries
and both deficits are already strict there.  If \(q_\ast=1\),
\textup{(F4)} forces \(q=1\).  The endpoint, triangular-intersection, and
identifiability statements follow.
\end{proof}

The generalized-Erlang representation and its Coxian minimality theory are due
to He--Zhang \cite{HeZhang2009GeneralizedErlang,HeZhang2008MinimalCoxian}.
The following corollary is their serial phase-type structure specialized to
finitely many sampled Palm-tail coordinates; the confluent calculation records
how repeated rates remain recoverable in that specialization.

\begin{corollary}[Finite-coordinate serial specialization with collisions]
\label{cor:nstate-serial-all-collision-identifiability-main}
Fix \(n\ge2\), a known \(\Delta\tau>0\), and arbitrary positive rates
\(\theta=(\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_n)\), with repetitions allowed. Let
\[
Q(\theta)_{ii}=-\theta_i,\qquad
Q(\theta)_{i,i-1}=\theta_i\quad(2\le i\le n),
\]
with all other entries zero, and put
\[
T_0(\theta)=e^{\Delta\tau Q(\theta)},\qquad
T_1(\theta)=(I-T_0(\theta))\mathbf1e_n^\top.
\]
Then the visible record identifies exactly the unordered rate multiset
\(\{\!\{\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_n\}\!\}\). Equivalently, the fibre of the
labelled serial parameter is its \(\mathfrak S_n\)-permutation orbit.

More explicitly, if the distinct rates are
\(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r\) with multiplicities
\(m_1,\ldots,m_r\), and \(z_j=e^{-\lambda_j\Delta\tau}\), then the Palm tail
has the confluent Prony form
\[
S_k=\sum_{j=1}^r\sum_{\ell=0}^{m_j-1}
c_{j\ell}k^\ell z_j^k,\qquad c_{j,m_j-1}\ne0,
\]
and its minimal recurrence polynomial is
\[
\chi_\theta(x)=\prod_{j=1}^r(x-z_j)^{m_j}.
\]
The leading \(n\times n\) Hankel matrix
\((S_{i+j})_{0\le i,j<n}\) is invertible; hence
\(S_0,\ldots,S_{2n-1}\) determine \(\chi_\theta\), including all collision
multiplicities. Pole collision therefore changes conditioning and the local
root chart, but does not change population identifiability.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Starting in phase \(n\), the absorption time is
\(W=X_n+\cdots+X_1\), where the \(X_i\) are independent exponentials with
rates \(\theta_i\). Its Laplace transform is
\[
\EE e^{-sW}=\prod_{i=1}^n\frac{\theta_i}{s+\theta_i}.
\]
Consequently
\[
\int_0^\infty e^{-st}\PP(W>t)\,dt
=\frac{1-\prod_{i=1}^n\theta_i/(s+\theta_i)}{s}
\]
has at
\(-\lambda_j\) a pole of exact order \(m_j\). Partial-fraction inversion and
sampling at \(t=k\Delta\tau\) give the displayed exponential-polynomial form
with nonzero top confluent coefficient. Standard confluent Prony algebra
then gives the exact minimal annihilator \(\chi_\theta\)
\cite{KunisRoemerVonDerOhe2020Prony}.

For completeness of the finite-coordinate assertion, the matrices
\(\mathcal O(Q)\) and \(\mathcal C(Q)\) are nonsingular: successive rows
\(e_n^\top Q^j\) and successive columns \(Q^j\mathbf1\) expose the nonzero
bidiagonal products triangularly. Because the exponential is injective on
the real spectrum of \(Q\), Hermite interpolation supplies a polynomial
\(p\) with \(Q=p(e^{\Delta\tau Q})\); hence the corresponding observation and
reachability spaces for \(T_0=e^{\Delta\tau Q}\) are also full. Therefore
\[
(S_{i+j})_{0\le i,j<n}=\mathcal O(T_0)\mathcal C(T_0)
\]
is invertible, and the first \(2n\) tails solve uniquely for the order-\(n\)
recurrence. The roots and their algebraic multiplicities recover
\(\lambda_j=-\Delta\tau^{-1}\log z_j\) and \(m_j\). Conversely, permuting
the independent summands does not change \(W\), the Palm gap law, or the
stationary renewal law. This proves the exact fibre claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Quantitative interior fibre dimension and positivity persistence]
\label{thm:nstate-interior-continuum-nonidentifiability-main}
For the unrestricted class with \(\sim_{\mathcal K_n}\) equal to
reset-preserving state relabelling, the hidden kernel is locally
nonidentifiable at every minimal interior point \(K\), where interior means
\(K>0\) and \((I-K)\mathbf1>0\) entrywise. More precisely, the
reset-preserving similarity group has Lie algebra
\[
\mathfrak g_n=\{B:B\mathbf1=0,\ \beta B=0\},
\qquad \dim\mathfrak g_n=(n-1)^2,
\]
and the orbit map
\[
\psi_K:\mathcal G_n\longrightarrow\mathbb R^{n\times n},\qquad
\psi_K(M)=M^{-1}KM,
\]
has injective differential
\[
D\psi_K(I)B=[K,B]\qquad(B\in\mathfrak g_n).
\]
Consequently the visible fibre through \(K\) contains, in a neighbourhood of
\(K\), a smooth immersed manifold of dimension exactly \((n-1)^2\).  Every
kernel on a sufficiently small neighbourhood of this orbit manifold remains
strictly substochastic: its entries and killing deficits are positive.  After
quotienting by the finite reset-preserving permutation group, the local fibre
still has dimension \((n-1)^2\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Because \(\beta\mathbf1=1\),
\(\RR^n=\operatorname{span}\{\mathbf1\}\oplus\ker\beta\).  Relative to this
decomposition, \(B\mathbf1=0\) removes the first block column and
\(\beta B=0\) forces the range into \(\ker\beta\).  Thus
\(B=\operatorname{diag}(0,A)\), with arbitrary
\(A\in\operatorname{End}(\ker\beta)\), and
\(\dim\mathfrak g_n=(n-1)^2\).

Differentiating \(\exp(-tB)K\exp(tB)\) at zero gives
\(D\psi_K(I)B=[K,B]\).  Suppose \([K,B]=0\).  Since \(B\mathbf1=0\),
\[
BK^j\mathbf1=K^jB\mathbf1=0,
\qquad j=0,\ldots,n-1.
\]
The reachability matrix
\([\mathbf1,K\mathbf1,\ldots,K^{n-1}\mathbf1]\) has rank \(n\), so these
equalities imply \(B=0\).  Hence the differential is injective on all of
\(\mathfrak g_n\), not merely in one selected direction.

The constant-rank theorem now makes the image of \(\psi_K\) near the identity
a smooth immersed manifold of dimension \((n-1)^2\).  For every
\(M\in\mathcal G_n\),
\[
\beta\psi_K(M)^j\mathbf1=\beta K^j\mathbf1\qquad(j\ge0),
\]
so the recalled orbit-fibre result places this entire manifold in the visible
fibre.  Finally, all entries of \(M^{-1}KM\) and all components of
\((I-M^{-1}KM)\mathbf1\) depend continuously on \(M\).  Their values at
\(M=I\) are strictly positive; a sufficiently small identity neighbourhood
therefore remains inside the interior Markovian cone.  The stabilizer has zero
Lie algebra by injectivity of the differential, and quotienting by the finite
reset-preserving permutation group cannot change local dimension.  This proves
all assertions.

For a physical root pair,
\(\mathcal D(r_N)=(p_N-s_N)^2\) and \(e(r_N)=0\), proving the physical-side
claim.  On the nonreal side, \(b_*<0\) for \(\xi>0\).  Coupling
\(Y=Z+b_*\) to the boundary variable \(Z\) shows that \(Y^-\) strictly

strictly greater power.  At \(\xi=0\) the laws coincide.  The tangent-cone
formulas follow from the rank-two constraint qualification and the local
description \(e=0,\mathcal D\ge0\).
\end{proof}

\begin{paperthm}[Single-record complete visible-law specification test]
\label{thm:complete-visible-law-specification-main}
Fix $0<\varepsilon<M<\infty$, put

\[
\Theta_{\varepsilon,M}=[\varepsilon,M]^2,
\qquad \mathcal P_0=\{P_\theta:\theta\in\Theta_{\varepsilon,M}\},
\]

where $P_\theta$ is the stationary visible law of the sampled-counter kernels
at the dimensionless parameter $\theta=(x,y)$.  For a binary word
$w=(w_1,\ldots,w_m)$, write

\[
P_\theta[w]=\pi_\theta T_{w_1}(\theta)\cdots
T_{w_m}(\theta)\mathbf1,
\]

where $\pi_\theta$ is stationary for $T_0(\theta)+T_1(\theta)$.  On
stationary binary laws define

\[
d(P,Q)=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}4^{-m}
\sum_{w\in\{0,1\}^m}|P[w]-Q[w]|,
\qquad
d(P,\mathcal P_0)=\inf_{\theta\in\Theta_{\varepsilon,M}}d(P,P_\theta).
\]

For a record $A_0,\ldots,A_{N-1}$ with $N\ge3$, let

\[
\widehat P_N[w]=\frac1{N-m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{N-m}
\mathbf1_{\{A_j\cdots A_{j+m-1}=w\}},
\qquad
L_N=\max\left\{3,\left\lfloor\frac18\log_2N\right\rfloor\right\},
\]

and set

\[
d_N=\min_{\theta\in\Theta_{\varepsilon,M}}
\sum_{m=1}^{L_N}4^{-m}\sum_{w\in\{0,1\}^m}
|\widehat P_N[w]-P_\theta[w]|.
\]

Choose a minimizer $\widehat\theta_N$ by a fixed lexicographic rule.  Delete
the initial and terminal incomplete cycles and denote the remaining complete
zero gaps by $G_1,\ldots,G_{K_N}$.  If $g_\theta$ is the model Palm gap
mass function, define

\[
h_\theta(k)=\mathbf1_{\{k=1\}}
-\frac{g_\theta(1)}{g_\theta(2)}\mathbf1_{\{k=2\}}.
\]

When $K_N\ge3$, put

\[
\widehat\nu_N=\frac1{K_N}\sum_{j=1}^{K_N}
h_{\widehat\theta_N}(G_j)^2,
\qquad
\widehat I_N=\frac{K_N}{N}\widehat\nu_N^2,
\]

\[
Z_N=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\widehat I_N}}
\sum_{j=1}^{K_N-1}
h_{\widehat\theta_N}(G_j)h_{\widehat\theta_N}(G_{j+1}).
\]

If $K_N<3$ or $\widehat I_N=0$, set $Z_N=0$.  Thus, for every binary
record of length $N\ge3$, the test

\[
\varphi_N=\mathbf1_{\{d_N>N^{-1/4}\ \text{or}\ Z_N>z_{1-\alpha}\}}
\tag{F}
\]

is defined without empirical roots, logarithms, or evaluations of the analytic
root chart outside its domain.  It has the following properties.

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item Its null size is compact-uniform:
\[
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\varepsilon,M}}
\left|\PP_\theta(\varphi_N=1)-\alpha\right|\longrightarrow0.
\]
This includes the exchange diagonal and every null triangular array, in
particular arrays satisfying $N^{1/4}|p_N-s_N|=O(1)$.

\item If $P$ is a fixed stationary ergodic binary law and
$d(P,\mathcal P_0)>0$, then
\[
\PP_P(\varphi_N=1)\longrightarrow1.
\]
The convergence is uniform on any class with a common geometric
$\alpha$-mixing envelope $\alpha_P(k)\le Cq^k$, $q<1$, and a common
separation $d(P,\mathcal P_0)\ge\delta>0$.  No consistency assertion is made
for arbitrary stationary nonergodic mixtures.

\item Fix an interior exchange point $\theta_0=(x_0,x_0)$, and abbreviate
$g_k=g_{\theta_0}(k)$, $\mu=\sum_{k\ge0}(k+1)g_k$, and
\[
h(k)=\mathbf1_{\{k=1\}}-\frac{g_1}{g_2}\mathbf1_{\{k=2\}}.
\]
For $0\le\eta<g_2/g_1$, the transition matrix
\[
Q_\eta(\ell\mid k)=g_\ell\{1+\eta h(k)h(\ell)\}
\tag{G}
\]

exec
"C:\\Windows\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command '$x=Get-Content -Encoding utf8 '"'article_fixed_inference.tex'; "'$x[560..675]' in D:\omega\automath\papers\publication\2026_detector_shells_click_record_kms_jphyscomm
 succeeded in 158ms:
\]
is stochastic, has stationary marginal $g$, and defines by Palm inversion a
stationary nonrenewal visible law when $\eta>0$.  This law preserves exactly
the sampled-counter coordinates $r_0,r_1,r_2$, because
\[
r_0=\mu^{-1},\qquad r_1=\mu^{-1}g_0,
\qquad r_2=\mu^{-1}\{g_1+g_0Q_\eta(0\mid0)\},
\]
and $Q_\eta(0\mid0)=g_0$.  Nevertheless,
\[
\PP_\eta(10101)=\mu^{-1}g_1^2(1+\eta),
\]
so a length-five word separates every $\eta>0$ from the renewal null.

\item Put
\[
\nu=\EE_g h(G)^2=g_1+\frac{g_1^2}{g_2},
\qquad \mathcal I=\frac{\nu^2}{\mu}.
\]
Along $\eta_N=t/\sqrt N$, $t\ge0$, the stationary record experiments in
part~\textup{(iii)} satisfy
\[
\log\frac{dP_{\eta_N}^{(N)}}{dP_0^{(N)}}
=t\Delta_N-\frac12t^2\mathcal I+o_{P_0}(1),
\qquad \Delta_N\Rightarrow N(0,\mathcal I).
\]
Moreover, $\widehat I_N\to_P\mathcal I$ and
\[
Z_N\Rightarrow N(t\sqrt{\mathcal I},1),
\qquad
\pi_\alpha(t)=1-\Phi_{\rm N}
\{z_{1-\alpha}-t\sqrt{\mathcal I}\}.
\]
Thus $\pi_\alpha(t)>\alpha$ for every $t>0$.  If
$\eta_N=o(N^{-1/2})$, the Kullback--Leibler divergence tends to zero, while
the score component of \textup{(F)} attains the Neyman--Pearson power envelope
of this one-dimensional Gaussian-shift subexperiment at the $N^{-1/2}$
rate.
\end{enumerate}

The distributional-distance guard in parts~\textup{(i)}--\textup{(ii)} is a
specialization of established stationary-ergodic testing methods
\cite{RyabkoRyabko2010ErgodicInference,Ryabko2012StationaryTesting}.  Generic
LAN and Gaussian-shift optimality are also standard
\cite{vanDerVaart1998AsymptoticStatistics}.  The model-specific assertions are
the exchange-uniform finite-range score, the exactly
$(r_0,r_1,r_2)$-preserving alternative \textup{(G)}, and their joint use with
the complete visible-law guard.
\end{paperthm}

\begin{proof}
On $\Theta_{\varepsilon,M}$, the hidden transition matrix is
\[
T_0+T_1=\begin{pmatrix}p&1-p\\b&1-b\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Each entry has a positive parameter-independent lower bound.  The hidden chain
therefore satisfies a uniform Doeblin condition, and every bounded function of
the visible record has a common geometric mixing envelope.  For a word $w$
of length $m\le N$, overlap for lags below $m$ and the mixing covariance
bound above that lag give
\[
\sup_\theta\operatorname{Var}_\theta\{\widehat P_N[w]\}
\le \frac{Cm}{N}.
\]
Consequently, by Cauchy--Schwarz,
\[
\sup_\theta\EE_\theta
\sum_{m=1}^{L_N}4^{-m}\sum_w
|\widehat P_N[w]-P_\theta[w]|
\le \frac{C'}{\sqrt N}
\sum_{m\ge1}2^{-m}\sqrt m=O(N^{-1/2}).
\]
Since the minimum defining $d_N$ is no larger than its value at the true
parameter, Markov's inequality yields
\[
\sup_\theta\PP_\theta(d_N>N^{-1/4})=O(N^{-1/4}).
\tag{1}
\]

Let $\widehat\sigma_N=(\widehat p_N+\widehat s_N,
\widehat p_N\widehat s_N)$ and $\sigma_\theta=(p+s,ps)$.  Define the empirical
inclusion vector
\[
\widehat r_N^{\rm word}
=\{\widehat P_N[1],\widehat P_N[11],
\widehat P_N[101]+\widehat P_N[111]\}^\top.
\]
For every model parameter $\vartheta$, the corresponding coordinates obey
$r_0=P_\vartheta[1]$, $r_1=P_\vartheta[11]$, and
$r_2=P_\vartheta[101]+P_\vartheta[111]$.  Since all these words have length at
most three, the definition of the truncated objective gives the deterministic
bound
\[
\|\widehat r_N^{\rm word}-r(\widehat\theta_N)\|
\le C_0d_N.
\]
Consequently, for the true parameter $\theta$,
\[
\|r(\widehat\theta_N)-r(\theta)\|
\le C_0d_N+C_1\sum_{m\le3}\sum_{|w|=m}
|\widehat P_N[w]-P_\theta[w]|.
\tag{2a}
\]
The first part of the proof gives $d_N=O_{P_\theta}(N^{-1/2})$ uniformly,
and the finite sum in \textup{(2a)} has the same uniform rate.  The uniformly
bounded derivative of $\Phi$ on $\Theta_{\varepsilon,M}$ therefore gives
\[
\widehat\sigma_N-\sigma_\theta=O_{P_\theta}(N^{-1/2})
\tag{2}
\]
uniformly.  No ordered-root map is used in this step.  For every fixed gap
index, $g_\theta(k)$ is a symmetric analytic function of the two roots;
compactness and $\inf_\theta g_\theta(2)>0$ make $h_\theta$ and its first
two $\sigma$-derivatives uniformly bounded.

Under the null, the complete Palm gaps are consecutive members of an i.i.d.

.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-333-\begin{proof}
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex:334:The preceding transcript is an outward-rounded directed interval evaluation of
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-335-the finite D-MAP covariance and quotient-sandwich formulas, with the exact
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-336-input singleton \([z]=[0.5,0.5]\).  Directed outward rounding at every
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-337-arithmetic operation ensures that the determinant enclosure contains the true determinant
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-338-\(\det\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)\).  Its lower endpoint is larger than \(1.37\), and
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-339-therefore
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-340-\[
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-341-[I_{\det}]\subset[1.37,\infty).
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-342-\]
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-343-Thus both eigenvalues are positive.  Moreover, for a positive definite
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-344-\(2\times2\) matrix,
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-345-\[
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-346-\lambda_{\min}=\frac{\det\Sigma_\sigma}{\lambda_{\max}}
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-347-\ge\frac{\det\Sigma_\sigma}{\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma}
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-348->\frac{1.372485374000321}{36.95298777813732}>0.037.
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-349-\]
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex:350:This gives the asserted rank and quantitative nondegeneracy.  Since the transcript is attached
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-351-only to the singleton input interval, it certifies no candidate other than
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-352-\(z=1/2\).  There is therefore no terminal subinterval list for a continuum
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-353-\(Z_{\rm cert}\) in this appendix; any later interval-valued certified domain
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-354-would have to print its terminal subintervals and margins separately.
.\app_diagonal_branch_certificate.tex-355-\end{proof}

.\sec_records_part10.tex:296:\begin{lemma}[Explicit lag-two variance and finite-pair nondegeneracy certificate]\label{lem:lag-two-variance-nondegenerate-main}
.\sec_records_part10.tex-297-Let \(g_k=\PP(G=k)\) be the full-support gap law from
.\sec_records_part10.tex-298-\Cref{lem:renewal-gap-main}, let \(\mu=\EE(G+1)=1/\rho\), and let
.\sec_records_part10.tex-299-\[
.\sec_records_part10.tex-300-f(i,j)=
.\sec_records_part10.tex-301-\mathbf1_{\{i=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{i=0,\ j=0\}}
.\sec_records_part10.tex-302--\rho\bigl(1+\mathbf1_{\{i\ge1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{j=0\}}\bigr)
.\sec_records_part10.tex-303-+(\rho^2-\gamma_2)(i+1),
.\sec_records_part10.tex-304-\qquad i,j\ge0 .
--
.\sec_records_part10.tex-318-It is also the absolutely convergent double/triple sum
.\sec_records_part10.tex-319-\begin{equation}\label{eq:lag-two-explicit-variance-main}
.\sec_records_part10.tex-320-\sigma_{\gamma_2}^2
.\sec_records_part10.tex-321-=\frac1{\mu}\left[
.\sec_records_part10.tex-322-\sum_{i,j\ge0}f(i,j)^2g_ig_j
.\sec_records_part10.tex-323-+2\sum_{i,j,k\ge0}f(i,j)f(j,k)g_ig_jg_k
.\sec_records_part10.tex-324-\right].
.\sec_records_part10.tex-325-\end{equation}
.\sec_records_part10.tex:326:Thus numerical verification of the studentizing variance is finite and
.\sec_records_part10.tex-327-monotone: truncate the two sums to \(0\le i,j,k\le K\), bound the omitted tail
.\sec_records_part10.tex-328-with any slack envelope \(g_k\le C_{\bar r}\bar r^k\),
.\sec_records_part10.tex-329-\(\bar r\in(\max\{p,s\},1)\), from
.\sec_records_part10.tex-330-\Cref{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main} and the linear growth of
.\sec_records_part10.tex-331-\(f\), and increase \(K\) until the tail bound is below the required tolerance.  The
.\sec_records_part10.tex:332:closed nondegeneracy certificate below shows that this numerical recipe is a
.\sec_records_part10.tex-333-check on arithmetic rather than an additional assumption.
.\sec_records_part10.tex-334-Equivalently, with
.\sec_records_part10.tex-335-\[
.\sec_records_part10.tex-336-R_i=\sum_{j\ge0}f(i,j)g_j,\qquad
.\sec_records_part10.tex-337-C_i=\sum_{j\ge0}f(j,i)g_j,\qquad
.\sec_records_part10.tex-338-M=\sum_{i,j\ge0}f(i,j)g_ig_j,
.\sec_records_part10.tex-339-\]
.\sec_records_part10.tex-340-one has the sum-of-squares form
.\sec_records_part10.tex-341-\begin{equation}\label{eq:lag-two-variance-squares-main}
.\sec_records_part10.tex-342-\mu\,\sigma_{\gamma_2}^2
.\sec_records_part10.tex-343-=\sum_{i\ge0}g_i(R_i+C_i-2M)^2
.\sec_records_part10.tex-344-+\sum_{i,j\ge0}g_ig_j\bigl(f(i,j)-R_i-C_j+M\bigr)^2 .
.\sec_records_part10.tex-345-\end{equation}
.\sec_records_part10.tex-346-The zero-variance criterion from \eqref{eq:row-column-zero-coboundary-main}
.\sec_records_part10.tex-347-reduces nondegeneracy to checking that such a coboundary cannot hold.  The
.\sec_records_part10.tex:348:finite certificate
.\sec_records_part10.tex:349:\begin{equation}\label{eq:lag-two-finite-nondegeneracy-certificate-main}
.\sec_records_part10.tex-350-f(0,0)-f(0,1)=1-\rho,\qquad
.\sec_records_part10.tex-351-f(1,0)-f(1,1)=-\rho
.\sec_records_part10.tex-352-\end{equation}
.\sec_records_part10.tex-353-will be compared after the zero-variance criterion is identified in the proof.
.\sec_records_part10.tex-354-It excludes the only possible zero-variance coboundary.  Hence
.\sec_records_part10.tex-355-the expression in \eqref{eq:lag-two-probabilistic-variance-main} is strictly
.\sec_records_part10.tex-356-positive.
.\sec_records_part10.tex-357-In particular \(\sigma_{\gamma_2}^2>0\) for every
--
.\sec_records_part10.tex-751-the quotient estimator and its covariance remain the visible-record output.

.\sec_records_part10.tex-754-\begin{proposition}[Quotient-only estimands from one constrained D-MAP fixed-setting long record]\label{thm:one-record-estimates-main}
.\sec_records_part10.tex-755-Under \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}, a single fixed-setting

.\sec_records_part3.tex-83-dimensionless products \(\{-\log p,-\log s\}\).  Finally,
.\sec_records_part3.tex-84-\Cref{lem:gap-law-exchange-symmetry-main} proves that swapping
.\sec_records_part3.tex-85-the two roots also leaves the stationary renewal gap law, and hence all
.\sec_records_part3.tex-86-finite-dimensional visible distributions, unchanged.  The inverse therefore
.\sec_records_part3.tex-87-lands on the quotient by the exchange \((p,\Gamma)\leftrightarrow
.\sec_records_part3.tex-88-(s,\kappa_{\mathrm r})\), not on separated labelled rates.  At a double root

.\sec_records_part8.tex-161-\begin{proposition}[Pre-diagonal quotient delta method]\label{prop:finite-record-pre-diagonal-quotient-rule-main}
.\sec_records_part8.tex-162-Let \(\Rinch_N\) be any three-coordinate finite-record estimator.  On the
.\sec_records_part8.tex-163-population inverse domain
.\sec_records_part8.tex-164-\[
.\sec_records_part8.tex-165-r_0>0,\qquad \Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}(\Rinc):=r_1-r_0^2\ne0,

.\sec_records_part8.tex-215-\]

.\sec_records_part8.tex-217-\[
.\sec_records_part8.tex-218-z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2=0
.\sec_records_part8.tex-219-\]
.\sec_records_part8.tex-220-are locally \(C^1\) functions of \(\sigma=\Phi(\Rinc)\).  The unordered
.\sec_records_part8.tex-221-survival-factor set \(\{p,s\}\) is therefore identified locally from
.\sec_records_part8.tex-222-\(\Phi(\Rinc)\).  For known design interval \(\Delta\tau\), the unordered
.\sec_records_part8.tex:223:numerical rate set follows by applying
.\sec_records_part8.tex-224-\[
.\sec_records_part8.tex-225-z\longmapsto -\frac{\log z}{\Delta\tau}
.\sec_records_part8.tex-226-\]
.\sec_records_part8.tex-227-to the two roots.  Without known \(\Delta\tau\), the same roots identify only
.\sec_records_part8.tex-228-the dimensionless products \(\{-\log p,-\log s\}
.\sec_records_part8.tex-229-=\{\Gamma\Delta\tau,\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau\}\).  No diagonal
.\sec_records_part8.tex-230-projection, exchange-diagonal confidence, or diagonal no-label conclusion is
.\sec_records_part8.tex-231-asserted in this proposition.
--
.\sec_records_part8.tex-266-The first term is \(o_{\PP}(t_N)\); the second is \(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), hence
.\sec_records_part8.tex-267-\(o_{\PP}(t_N)\) by the stated threshold scale.
.\sec_records_part8.tex-268-
.\sec_records_part8.tex-269-If \(\Delta_\sigma>0\), the discriminant is nonzero and the implicit-function
.\sec_records_part8.tex-270-theorem applied to \(z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2\) gives two local \(C^1\) root
.\sec_records_part8.tex-271-charts; taking the set of their values gives the unordered root map, independent
.\sec_records_part8.tex-272-of a branch labelling.  The logarithm map is \(C^1\) on compact subintervals of
.\sec_records_part8.tex-273-\((0,1)\), so, for known design interval \(\Delta\tau\), the unordered
.\sec_records_part8.tex:274:numerical rate set is the image of this unordered root set under
.\sec_records_part8.tex-275-\(z\mapsto-\log z/\Delta\tau\).  Without known \(\Delta\tau\), the same root

.\sec_records_part6.tex-76-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-77-and let \(Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma)\), where \(\Sigma\) is a positive
.\sec_records_part6.tex-78-definite \(2\times2\) matrix.  For \(c\in\mathbb R\), define
--
.\sec_records_part6.tex-151-\{u\in\operatorname{int}H_z:a<u^\top\Sigma^{-1}u<b\}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-152-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-153-is nonempty.  On this set \(T_{z,\Sigma}=u^\top\Sigma^{-1}u\), and the
.\sec_records_part6.tex-154-full-rank Gaussian density is strictly positive.  Hence
.\sec_records_part6.tex-155-\(\PP\{a<T_{z,\Sigma}<b\}>0\), which proves the claimed strict increase.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-156-\end{proof}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-157-
.\sec_records_part6.tex-158-\ifdefined\SUPPLEMENTARYFULLPROTOCOLS
.\sec_records_part6.tex:159:\begin{algorithmiclemma}[Transcript certificate for a finite diagonal comparison]\label{thm:certified-diagonal-inference-main}\label{prop:projected-gaussian-certificate-interface-main}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-160-Fix \(0<\alpha<1\), put \(q_\alpha=1-\alpha\), and fix a reported exchange-diagonal
.\sec_records_part6.tex-161-candidate \(z_\star\in(0,1)\).  A theorem-level finite diagonal comparison
.\sec_records_part6.tex:162:certificate for this candidate is the single finite tuple
.\sec_records_part6.tex-163-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-164-\mathcal C(z_\star,\alpha,N)
.\sec_records_part6.tex-165-=\bigl([z],[\theta],[\Sigma_\sigma],[I_\tau],
.\sec_records_part6.tex-166-[I_{\det}],[w],[L,U],[Q]\bigr),
.\sec_records_part6.tex-167-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-168-together with the branch-specific auxiliary interval \([q]\) or
.\sec_records_part6.tex-169-\([a_{\rm rn}]\) only in the rank-one non-normal case.  The tuple is mandatory:
.\sec_records_part6.tex-170-without the branch entries, the critical-value bracket, and the statistic
.\sec_records_part6.tex:171:enclosure in the same displayed transcript, the article has only an
.\sec_records_part6.tex:172:asymptotic theorem or a diagnostic calculation, not a finite transcript
.\sec_records_part6.tex-173-validating a realized accept/reject/undecided output.  The tuple entries have
.\sec_records_part6.tex-174-the following meanings:
.\sec_records_part6.tex-175-\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.4em,itemsep=0.25em]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-176-\item a point or interval input enclosure \([z]\Subset(0,1)\) with
.\sec_records_part6.tex-177-\(z_\star\in[z]\), and an outward-rounded enclosure \([\theta]\) of
.\sec_records_part6.tex-178-\(\theta=-\log z_\star\);
.\sec_records_part6.tex-179-\item outward-rounded interval enclosures for the entries of the finite
.\sec_records_part6.tex-180-D-MAP covariance
--
.\sec_records_part6.tex-186-\item the branch intervals
.\sec_records_part6.tex-187-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-188-[I_\tau]\supset\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(z_\star),\qquad
.\sec_records_part6.tex-189-[I_{\det}]\supset\det\Sigma_\sigma(z_\star),\qquad
.\sec_records_part6.tex-190-[w]\supset\Sigma_\sigma(z_\star)(1,z_\star)^\top,
.\sec_records_part6.tex-191-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-192-and, when needed for the rank-one non-normal branch, the scale enclosure
.\sec_records_part6.tex-193-\([a_{\rm rn}]\) of
.\sec_records_part6.tex:194:\eqref{eq:diagonal-rank-one-nonnormal-scale-certificate-main};
.\sec_records_part6.tex-195-\item an explicit rank margin \(\eta>0\) proving exactly one of the branch
.\sec_records_part6.tex:196:alternatives in \Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main};
.\sec_records_part6.tex-197-\item an outward-rounded statistic enclosure \([Q]=[[Q]^-,[Q]^+]\) with
.\sec_records_part6.tex-198-\(Q(z_\star)\in[Q]\);
.\sec_records_part6.tex-199-\item a certified critical-value bracket \([L,U]\), \(L<U\), for the same
.\sec_records_part6.tex-200-branch and the same candidate, obtained either from the certified ideal-CDF
.\sec_records_part6.tex-201-route or from a one-sided DKW simulation route.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-202-\end{enumerate}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-203-Thus all branch, quantile, and statistic comparisons are made by the single
.\sec_records_part6.tex-204-object \(\mathcal C(z_\star,\alpha,N)\); algorithms may produce its entries,
.\sec_records_part6.tex:205:but no algorithmic intermediate is itself a theorem-level certificate unless
.\sec_records_part6.tex-206-it appears in this tuple or in the stated rank-one non-normal addendum.  In
.\sec_records_part6.tex:207:particular, a printed population branch certificate without \([L,U]\) and
.\sec_records_part6.tex-208-\([Q]\) does not license a finite comparison for a realized record.  The branch
.\sec_records_part6.tex-209-item is passed only by one of the four mutually exclusive tests:
.\sec_records_part6.tex-210-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-211-[I_{\det}]\subset[\eta,\infty)\quad\text{(full rank),}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-212-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-213-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-214-[I_{\det}]=[0,0],\quad [I_\tau]\subset[\eta,\infty),\quad
.\sec_records_part6.tex-215-[w]=\{0\}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-216-\quad\text{(rank one, normal range),}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-217-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-218-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-219-[I_{\det}]=[0,0],\quad [I_\tau]\subset[\eta,\infty),\quad
.\sec_records_part6.tex-220-[q]\subset[\eta,\infty),\quad [a_{\rm rn}]\Subset(0,1]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-221-\quad\text{(rank one, non-normal range),}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-222-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-223-or exact entrywise \(\Sigma_\sigma(z_\star)=0\) for the zero-covariance
.\sec_records_part6.tex:224:branch.  If none of these finite tests is certified, the transcript status is
.\sec_records_part6.tex-225-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-226-\text{\rm undecided: branch not certified}.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-227-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-228-
.\sec_records_part6.tex:229:For an ideal-CDF transcript, let \(F_{z_\star,\Sigma}\) denote the
.\sec_records_part6.tex-230-projected-Gaussian CDF for the certified branch covariance \(\Sigma\), using
.\sec_records_part6.tex-231-the full-rank formula of
.\sec_records_part6.tex-232-\Cref{lem:deterministic-full-rank-projected-gaussian-quantile-main}, the
.\sec_records_part6.tex-233-rank-one non-normal formula
.\sec_records_part6.tex-234-\eqref{eq:rank-one-nonnormal-cdf-main}, the rank-one normal mixture in
.\sec_records_part6.tex-235-\Cref{thm:projected-gaussian-continuity-degeneracy-main}, or the
.\sec_records_part6.tex:236:zero-covariance degenerate law as appropriate.  The critical-value transcript
.\sec_records_part6.tex-237-must contain outward-rounded CDF enclosures satisfying
.\sec_records_part6.tex-238-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-239-F^-(c)\le F_{z_\star,\Sigma}(c)\le F^+(c)
.\sec_records_part6.tex-240-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-241-at its displayed rational endpoints and
.\sec_records_part6.tex-242-\begin{equation}\label{eq:certified-diagonal-critical-bracket-main}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-243-F^+(L)<q_\alpha\le F^-(U).
.\sec_records_part6.tex-244-\end{equation}
--
.\sec_records_part6.tex-258-Then the exact lower projected-Gaussian critical value
.\sec_records_part6.tex-259-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-260-c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma):=\inf\{c:F_{z_\star,\Sigma}(c)\ge q_\alpha\}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-261-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-262-satisfies
.\sec_records_part6.tex-263-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-264-L<c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma)\le U.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-265-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex:266:Consequently the finite theorem-level transcript validates exactly the
.\sec_records_part6.tex-267-following comparison:
.\sec_records_part6.tex-268-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-269-[Q]^+\le L
.\sec_records_part6.tex-270-\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
.\sec_records_part6.tex:271:\text{\rm transcript-valid accept of } Q(z_\star)\le c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma),
.\sec_records_part6.tex-272-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-273-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-274-[Q]^->U
.\sec_records_part6.tex-275-\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
.\sec_records_part6.tex:276:\text{\rm transcript-valid reject of } Q(z_\star)\le c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma),
.\sec_records_part6.tex-277-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-278-and in every remaining case
.\sec_records_part6.tex-279-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex:280:\text{\rm transcript-valid undecided: } Q(z_\star)-c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma)
.\sec_records_part6.tex-281-\in [[Q]^--U,\,[Q]^+-L].
.\sec_records_part6.tex-282-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-283-A midpoint \(m\in(L,U)\) for which \(F^-(m)<q_\alpha\le F^+(m)\) is therefore only
.\sec_records_part6.tex-284-an undecided midpoint inside a retained critical-value bracket; it is not an
.\sec_records_part6.tex:285:equality certificate and not an accept/reject decision.  This transcript
.\sec_records_part6.tex-286-validation theorem is separate from the fixed-null probability theorem
.\sec_records_part6.tex-287-\Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}: Theorem~E supplies an
.\sec_records_part6.tex-288-asymptotic ideal conditional critical value, while
.\sec_records_part6.tex-289-\(\mathcal C(z_\star,\alpha,N)\) is the additional finite record-specific
.\sec_records_part6.tex-290-evidence needed before any realized comparison can be printed.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-291-
.\sec_records_part6.tex:292:For a DKW simulation transcript, the supplied critical value \(\widehat c\)
.\sec_records_part6.tex-293-must satisfy the displayed one-sided auxiliary domination statement
.\sec_records_part6.tex-294-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-295-\PP^\ast\{\widehat c\ge c^{-}_{q_\alpha,N}(z_\star)\}\ge1-\delta_N,
.\sec_records_part6.tex-296-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-297-where \(c^{-}_{q_\alpha,N}(z_\star)\) is the ideal lower conditional
.\sec_records_part6.tex-298-projected-Gaussian quantile for the same retained branch.  Conditional on that
.\sec_records_part6.tex:299:auxiliary domination event, the exact finite transcript implication is
.\sec_records_part6.tex-300-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex:301:[Q]^+\le\widehat c\Rightarrow\text{\rm transcript-valid DKW accept},\qquad
.\sec_records_part6.tex:302:[Q]^->\widehat c\Rightarrow\text{\rm transcript-valid DKW reject},
.\sec_records_part6.tex-303-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-304-and otherwise \(\text{\rm undecided}\).  The probability attached to this
.\sec_records_part6.tex-305-route is only the stated auxiliary \(P_N^\ast\) domination probability; record
.\sec_records_part6.tex-306-coverage is obtained only by combining that product-space auxiliary statement
.\sec_records_part6.tex-307-with the fixed-null asymptotic event of
.\sec_records_part6.tex-308-\Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-309-
.\sec_records_part6.tex-310-If the candidate is not pre-specified, if \(z_\star\notin[z]\), if any branch
.\sec_records_part6.tex-311-interval or rank margin is absent, if the critical-value bracket is for a
.\sec_records_part6.tex:312:different candidate or branch, or if \([Q]\) is missing, the transcript status
.\sec_records_part6.tex-313-is only
.\sec_records_part6.tex-314-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-315-\text{\rm undecided: no certified finite diagonal inference}.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-316-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex:317:In the submitted manuscript the only attached paper-level branch certificate
.\sec_records_part6.tex:318:is the singleton certificate \(z_\star=1/2\) in
.\sec_records_part6.tex:319:\Cref{lem:z-half-diagonal-branch-certificate-main}; all other diagonal
.\sec_records_part6.tex-320-candidate displays are diagnostics unless they attach their own finite
.\sec_records_part6.tex:321:certificate data satisfying this theorem.  In particular, the singleton
.\sec_records_part6.tex:322:branch transcript in
.\sec_records_part6.tex:323:\Cref{app:self-contained-diagonal-branch-certificate-main} is not this tuple:
.\sec_records_part6.tex-324-it has \([z]\), \([\theta]\), \([\Sigma_\sigma]\), \([I_\tau]\),
.\sec_records_part6.tex-325-\([I_{\det}]\), and \([w]\), but deliberately has no record-specific
.\sec_records_part6.tex-326-\([L,U]\) and no realized statistic enclosure \([Q]\).
.\sec_records_part6.tex-327-\end{algorithmiclemma}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-328-
.\sec_records_part6.tex-329-\begin{proof}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-330-The branch portion is exactly the finite rank/range classification certified
.\sec_records_part6.tex:331:by \Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main} and justified by
.\sec_records_part6.tex:332:\Cref{lem:diagonal-interval-branch-certificate-main,lem:dmap-diagonal-covariance-alternatives-main}.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-333-The interval enclosures contain the actual covariance and branch quantities,
.\sec_records_part6.tex-334-and the margin \(\eta\) or exact zero identities select one population branch.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-335-Without such a branch, the projected-Gaussian law used by the critical-value
.\sec_records_part6.tex-336-calculation is not certified, so no accept/reject implication is licensed.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-337-
.\sec_records_part6.tex:338:Assume first that the ideal-CDF transcript is attached.  From
.\sec_records_part6.tex-339-\eqref{eq:certified-diagonal-critical-bracket-main},
.\sec_records_part6.tex-340-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-341-F_{z_\star,\Sigma}(L)\le F^+(L)<q_\alpha
.\sec_records_part6.tex-342-\le F^-(U)\le F_{z_\star,\Sigma}(U).
.\sec_records_part6.tex-343-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-344-By the definition of the lower quantile this implies
.\sec_records_part6.tex-345-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-346-L<c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma)\le U.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-347-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-348-Since \(Q(z_\star)\in[Q]\), the implication
.\sec_records_part6.tex-349-\([Q]^+\le L\) gives \(Q(z_\star)<c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma)\), and the implication
.\sec_records_part6.tex-350-\([Q]^->U\) gives \(Q(z_\star)>c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma)\).  If neither separation
.\sec_records_part6.tex-351-holds, the certified information only bounds the difference by
.\sec_records_part6.tex-352-\([[Q]^--U,[Q]^+-L]\), which may contain zero; hence the only rigorous finite
.\sec_records_part6.tex:353:transcript output is undecided.  The certified CDF and quantile bracketing routines used
.\sec_records_part6.tex-354-to produce such endpoint inequalities are
.\sec_records_part6.tex-355-\Cref{thm:certified-projected-gaussian-quantile-algorithm-main,lem:certified-deterministic-projected-gaussian-quantile-main,lem:certified-rank-one-nonnormal-projected-gaussian-quantile-main};
.\sec_records_part6.tex-356-the rank-one normal and zero-covariance cases use the explicit laws stated in
.\sec_records_part6.tex-357-\Cref{thm:projected-gaussian-continuity-degeneracy-main}.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-358-
.\sec_records_part6.tex-359-For the simulated route, the displayed DKW statement is a one-sided
.\sec_records_part6.tex:360:certificate that the simulated critical value dominates the ideal conditional
.\sec_records_part6.tex-361-critical value on an auxiliary event of probability at least
.\sec_records_part6.tex-362-\(1-\delta_N\).  On that auxiliary event, the same interval comparison of
.\sec_records_part6.tex:363:\([Q]\) with the supplied critical value proves the stated transcript-valid
.\sec_records_part6.tex-364-accept, reject, or undecided implication.  The domination probability is conditional on the
.\sec_records_part6.tex-365-auxiliary Gaussian simulation, so its record-level interpretation is exactly
.\sec_records_part6.tex-366-the product-space statement proved in
.\sec_records_part6.tex-367-\Cref{lem:conditional-monte-carlo-quantile-main}, combined separately with
.\sec_records_part6.tex-368-\Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}; it is not a finite-sample
.\sec_records_part6.tex-369-coverage claim for the retained record alone.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-370-\end{proof}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-371-\else
.\sec_records_part6.tex:372:\begin{remark}[Supplementary finite-comparison certificate interface]\label{thm:certified-diagonal-inference-main}\label{prop:projected-gaussian-certificate-interface-main}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-373-The finite tuple that would be needed for a realized diagonal
.\sec_records_part6.tex-374-accept/reject/undecided comparison is supplementary implementation material,
.\sec_records_part6.tex-375-not part of the delivered main theorem chain.  The main article uses only the

.\sec_records_part6.tex-384-\begin{lemma}[Projected-Gaussian continuity and rank degeneracy]\label{thm:projected-gaussian-continuity-degeneracy-main}
--
.\sec_records_part6.tex-560-diagonal is the finite expression
.\sec_records_part6.tex-561-\begin{equation}\label{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-r-finite-main}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-562-\Sigma_r(z)=\frac1\mu\left\{
.\sec_records_part6.tex-563-A_z-e_zR^\top-Re_z^\top+\nu RR^\top
.\sec_records_part6.tex-564-+B_z-d_zR^\top+B_z^\top-Rd_z^\top
.\sec_records_part6.tex-565-\right\}.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-566-\end{equation}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-567-Consequently the actual diagonal covariance used by the projected-Gaussian
.\sec_records_part6.tex:568:statistic is the reproducible \(2\times2\) matrix
.\sec_records_part6.tex-569-\begin{equation}\label{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-570-\Sigma_\sigma(z)=D\Phi(\Rinc(z))\,\Sigma_r(z)\,D\Phi(\Rinc(z))^\top
.\sec_records_part6.tex-571-\end{equation}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-572-where \(D\Phi\) is the explicit Jacobian in
.\sec_records_part6.tex-573-\eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main}, evaluated at
.\sec_records_part6.tex-574-the displayed \(\Rinc(z)\).  No infinite covariance-tail truncation or simulation
.\sec_records_part6.tex-575-input is hidden in \eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main}: for a fixed
.\sec_records_part6.tex-576-\(z\), all entries are obtained by ordinary arithmetic from
--
.\sec_records_part6.tex-616-\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-r-finite-main}.  The last display is then the
.\sec_records_part6.tex-617-delta-method sandwich for the symmetric quotient coordinates
.\sec_records_part6.tex-618-\(\Phi(\Rinc(z))=(p+s,ps)\), whose Jacobian was computed in
.\sec_records_part6.tex-619-\eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main}.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-620-\end{proof}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-621-
.\sec_records_part6.tex-622-The main theorem uses
.\sec_records_part6.tex-623-\Cref{lem:dmap-diagonal-covariance-alternatives-main} only through the printed

.\sec_records_part6.tex:625:\Cref{app:self-contained-diagonal-branch-certificate-main}.  The complete
.\sec_records_part6.tex:626:interval transcript and the outward-rounded verifier are deliberately outside
.\sec_records_part6.tex-627-the mathematical flow of this section; see

.\sec_records_part6.tex-631-We now specialize the generic renewal tools to the standing record experiment
.\sec_records_part6.tex-632-of \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}.  The exponential-tail lemma
.\sec_records_part6.tex-633-verifies the renewal hypothesis for this model.  Theorem~C then states the
.\sec_records_part6.tex-634-exact finite-record statistic for the three inclusion coordinates and applies
.\sec_records_part6.tex-635-the generic complete-cycle CLT to that statistic.

.\sec_records_part6.tex-637-\begin{lemma}[Killed-leakage gap exponential moment]\label{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main}
.\sec_records_part6.tex-638-Under \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}, set
--
.\sec_records_part6.tex-658-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-659-0<\eta<-\log r,
.\sec_records_part6.tex-660-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex-661-the gap and cycle length satisfy
.\sec_records_part6.tex-662-\[
.\sec_records_part6.tex-663-\EE e^{\eta G}<\infty,\qquad
.\sec_records_part6.tex-664-\EE e^{\eta (G+1)}<\infty .
.\sec_records_part6.tex-665-\]
.\sec_records_part6.tex:666:Thus the regenerative exponential-moment hypothesis used below is a verified
.\sec_records_part6.tex-667-consequence of the killed-leakage model, not an additional assumption.
.\sec_records_part6.tex-668-\end{lemma}

.\supp_projection_example.tex-1-\begin{proposition}[Pointwise exchange-diagonal projection example]\label{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}
.\supp_projection_example.tex-2-Fix
.\supp_projection_example.tex-3-\[
.\supp_projection_example.tex-4-z_0=\frac12,\qquad \sigma_0=(1,1/4),\qquad
.\supp_projection_example.tex-5-H_{z_0}=\{h\in\mathbb R^2:h_2\le z_0h_1\},
.\supp_projection_example.tex-6-\]
.\supp_projection_example.tex-7-and let \(F=\{(\sigma_1,\sigma_2):\sigma_2\le\sigma_1^2/4\}\).  Suppose the
.\supp_projection_example.tex-8-true population satisfies \(\sigma=\sigma_0\), the printed interval
.\supp_projection_example.tex:9:certificate proves
.\supp_projection_example.tex-10-\(\lambda_{\min}\{\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)\}>0.037\), and
.\supp_projection_example.tex-11-\[
.\supp_projection_example.tex-12-\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}-\sigma_0)
.\supp_projection_example.tex-13-\Rightarrow Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)).
.\supp_projection_example.tex-14-\]
.\supp_projection_example.tex-15-Let \(\Pi_F\) be the locally unique Euclidean projection, with any fixed
.\supp_projection_example.tex-16-measurable tie rule outside the local neighbourhood, and set

# Reproducing the numerical checks

Run all commands from the paper directory. The stored run used Python 3.10.11,
NumPy 1.26.4, SciPy 1.12.0, and mpmath 1.3.0. Install the dependencies with:

```sh
python -m pip install "numpy==1.26.4" "scipy==1.12.0" "mpmath==1.3.0"
```

## Verification commands

```sh
python -m unittest discover -s artifacts -p "test_*.py" -v
python artifacts/verify_A8_results.py --output artifacts/verify_A8_results_output.txt
python artifacts/verify_nstate_identifiability.py --output artifacts/verify_nstate_identifiability_output.txt --two-state-output artifacts/verify_two_state_fibre_output.txt
```

The unit suite prints `Ran 53 tests` followed by `OK`. The first report begins
with `A8 sampled-counter verification` and ends with a finite tested
information range. The second begins with
`Sharp killed-reset D-MAP identifiability dichotomy verification` and ends with
the stated exact Markovian orbit-fibre boundary. The two-state option writes the
deterministic two-state diagnostic excerpt. Both verifiers exit zero.

All archived reports use Unix LF and contain no clock or date fields.

## Checksum verification

Paths in `SHA256SUMS` are relative to the `artifacts` directory. From the paper
directory, run exactly:

```sh
cd artifacts && sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS
```

A successful check prints exactly 10 `OK` lines and no failures. The
project-local `.gitattributes` pins every file covered by the manifest to LF.

\begin{remark}[Parry comparator in the supplement]
The ideal one-step blanking Parry benchmark is a comparator only.  It is not
part of the D-MAP theorem package and is recorded in the supplementary
material rather than as a main-text theorem or appendix.
\end{remark}

No specific funding was received for this work.

\section*{Data availability}

No empirical data were created or analysed.  The population covariance
certificate is independently replayable from
\texttt{certificates/diagonal\_branch\_certificate.py}; its stored transcript
is \texttt{certificates/diagonal\_branch\_z\_half.json}, and exact commands,
environment versions, scope, and SHA-256 hashes are recorded in
\texttt{certificates/README.md}.  The script uses only the Python standard
library and certifies only \(z_0=1/2\).  No simulations, fits, or validation
data are used as evidence for the theorem statements.

# Diagonal covariance certificate replay

This archive certifies only the preselected population point `z_0 = 1/2` used
in the manuscript's pointwise projection example. It does not certify a
parameter interval, a critical value, a finite-record statistic, or coverage.

Files:

- `diagonal_branch_certificate.py`: standard-library interval-arithmetic
  generator and unit tests.
- `diagonal_branch_z_half.json`: stored outward-rounded transcript.

Recorded replay environment:

- Python 3.10.11
- `decimal` 1.70
- `libmpdec` 2.5.1
- third-party dependencies: none

From the manuscript source directory, run:

```text
python certificates/diagonal_branch_certificate.py --test
python certificates/diagonal_branch_certificate.py --environment
python certificates/diagonal_branch_certificate.py --hash
```

The first command regenerates the transcript, compares it with the stored JSON,
checks the displayed covariance entries and determinant directly from the
printed formulas, and runs negative regression fixtures. The `--hash` command
prints the canonical LF-terminated JSON hash and byte length.

Immutable SHA-256 values for the submitted files:

```text
FB3006C0E2F5C44E754C78967BBF52696B241D2845231A1F28C0AA02DDF846A1  diagonal_branch_certificate.py
C469252C9AAD42B5358C2FA25623D96F17DCC19D6760F38ACBC698EB5270FF0B  diagonal_branch_z_half.json
```

Canonical regenerated transcript:

```text
61fa032f034e8aad38ab88b52e98071d89335847ad6fda397ca570bf4a3c7db9  13323 bytes
```

The raw JSON file hash can differ from the canonical regenerated hash if a
checkout changes line endings or adds a byte-order mark. The unit test compares
the parsed data after canonical serialization and therefore detects substantive
changes.

NUMERICAL CONCLUSION
- the complete physical n=2 Markovian fibre is the exact q interval
  between 1 and gamma/recovery, with rate-swapped physical endpoints.
- strict fibre-interior positivity holds off diagonal; the equal-rate
  interval collapses to the singleton q=1.
- physical n=2 inclusion triples satisfy the exact scalar image equation.
- the hidden-mode grid respects the sharp lower bound -exp(-2).
- the sampled-cycle mean agrees with the expansion through order delta^4.
- n=2: the known three-inclusion inverse recovers the unordered rate pair.
- n=2,3,4 serial subclasses: the confluent Hankel recurrence recovers
  the unordered sampled-pole multiset, including repeated poles.
- unrestricted killed-reset kernels: explicit reset-preserving similarity
  pairs have identical visible laws but different hidden kernels for n=2,3,4.
- pole collision is an ill-conditioning/singular-chart boundary, not an
  identifiability boundary; the exact boundary is the Markovian orbit fibre.
