+which preserves r_0,r_1,r_2 exactly but changes P(10101) by the factor 1+eta. Along eta_N=t/sqrt(N), the stationary-record likelihood is LAN with information I=(E_g h(G)^2)^2/mu, and the score attains the Gaussian-shift power envelope for this one-dimensional subexperiment.
+
+There is also a compact-uniform physical-image test based on e(r)=0 and D(r)>=0. After residualization its statistic is Z_e^2+(Z_d^-)^2. At separated roots its limit is chi-square_1; on the exchange diagonal it is (chi-square_1+chi-square_2)/2 in the chi-bar-square sense. A log-N selector has compact-uniform asymptotic size at most alpha over [epsilon,M]^2, including intermediate sequences where the selector need not settle, and the test is consistent against the stated full-support renewal alternatives with e(r)!=0 or D(r)<0 and positive-definite constraint covariance.
+
+5. Supplementary claims. At an interior exchange point, the manuscript starts from the Bickel-Kwon fixed-marginal Markov interaction space and proves that imposing preservation of r_0,r_1,r_2 removes exactly the atom direction q(0,0):
+
+  T={q in L^2(g x g): E(q|G_0)=E(q|G_1)=0, q(0,0)=0}.
+
+It constructs positive two-sided paths preserving g and all three inclusions exactly, obtains calendar-time information mu^{-1}Pi_T, fixed-dimensional retained-record LAN subject to an explicit endpoint condition, and a weighted infinite score test with pointwise power against every nonzero fixed direction and exchange-local null calibration.
+
+For the equal-rate tail S_j=p_0^j(1+x_0 j), its moving Helmert layer of dimension d_J=(J+1)^2-1 has a uniform Euclidean coupling to N(0,I_{d_J}) over N^{-1/2}-local physical nulls iff
+
+  n_N S_{J_N}^2 -> infinity,   n_N=N/mu.
+
+In the second-order band this is equivalent to
+
+  2x_0 J_N <= log n_N+2 log log n_N-omega_N
+
+for some omega_N->infinity; at a fixed critical correction the corner instead has a compound-Poisson obstruction. Finally, for a deterministic product experiment of m_N(z)=floor(N/mu_z) independent complete Palm gaps and a symmetric split p=z+hN^{-1/4}, s=z-hN^{-1/4}, uniformly for z in a compact subset of (0,1) and bounded h,
+
+  log(dP_{N,z,h}/dP_{N,z,0})
+    =h^2 Delta_{N,z}-(h^4/2)I(z)+o_P(1),
+  Delta_{N,z}=>N(0,I(z)).
+
+This identifies h^2, not its sign, and is expressly not a stationary-record endpoint equivalence or an exhaustion of all local directions. The pointwise projection result at z=1/2 is only a standard metric-projection example, not a test or confidence procedure.
+
+Imported results versus our residual claims must be kept separate. The D-MAP/MAP framework is due to Neuts, Lucantoni, and Alfa-Neuts. Zamparo supplies renewal-binary finite-law results and the finite-order Markov criterion. Geometric MAP2 covariance behaviour was already studied by Ramirez-Cobo and Carrizosa. Kalman minimal-realization uniqueness and its MAP formulations are standard; Ramirez-Cobo, Wiper, and Lillo established MAP2 nonidentifiability, and Ramirez-Cobo and Lillo gave a complete weak-equivalence classification for MAP2/MAP3. Meszaros and Telek gave canonical discrete MAP2/RAP2 coordinates. Generalized-Erlang, acyclic phase-type, Coxian minimality, repeated-pole, and representation theory come from Cumani, O'Cinneide, He-Zhang, and related literature; confluent Prony recovery is standard. Regenerative CLTs, batch means, the multivariate delta method, stationary-ergodic distributional-distance testing, chi-bar-square boundary theory, generic LAN, and metric-projection limits are imported. Bickel-Kwon supply the doubly centred known-marginal Markov tangent and additive projection. The Gaussian-approximation proof imports Meckes's Stein estimate and the Bolbotowski-Bouchitte Z_2/W_2 comparison. The N^{-1/4} loss-of-identifiability mechanism has general antecedents in locally conic and loss-of-identifiability theory.
+
+What we can honestly claim as ours is therefore narrower: the model-specific closed Palm law, hazard and exact dependence threshold; the sharp hidden-mode range and sampling expansion; the unusually low-order three-inclusion quotient formula and analytic physical image; the exact intersection of the weak-equivalence orbit with this killed-reset Markov cone; the first-2n sampled-tail formulation for the serial subclass; the reset-preserving subgroup dimension/positivity calculation; the complete-cycle bookkeeping and model-specific tests; the additional atom constraint, positive realizing paths and calendar scaling in the Markov-gap calculation; the particular equal-rate moving-layer boundary if its proof is correct; and the explicit complete-Palm-gap symmetric-split likelihood. Even the three-inclusion formula is still described in the paper only as a candidate original result pending a complete reduction against Meszaros-Telek canonical DMAP2 coordinates. If the fibre arc drops out immediately from Ramirez-Cobo-Lillo after imposing the reset constraints, it is only an explicit cone-intersection corollary. The serial identifiability statement is only a finite-coordinate sampled specialization of generalized-Erlang/Coxian theory. The (n-1)^2 result is a short Lie-orbit consequence, not a new general realization theorem.
+
+The priority position must be assessed against us, not in our favour: the earlier external review found that the previous version omitted or underweighted at least three direct antecedent groups that materially shrink the novelty boundary: Bickel-Kwon for the fixed-marginal Markov tangent, Ramirez-Cobo-Lillo and Meszaros-Telek for weak equivalence/canonical order-two representations, and He-Zhang and the generalized-Erlang/Coxian literature for serial identifiability. The current manuscript has added these comparisons and reduced its claims, but it has not proved that the three-inclusion quotient is absent from canonical DMAP2 coordinates.
+
+Machine-verification evidence has a deliberately limited role. The reproducibility archive records Python 3.10.11, NumPy 1.26.4, SciPy 1.12.0, and mpmath 1.3.0; its unit suite reports 53 tests, all passing. The finite regression scripts check the image equation, hidden-mode bound, small-Delta expansion, Markov-gap centring and inclusion preservation, length-five separation, finite tangent bases, Helmert orthogonality and covariance identities, selected coupling error terms, the singular-gap expansion, exact two-state fibre endpoints, serial n=2,3,4 Hankel recovery including collisions, and visible-law invariance under explicit reset-preserving similarities. Stored examples report image residuals around 10^{-16}, two-state survival-coordinate errors around 10^{-16}, and exact recovery of the tested unordered rate multisets. A standard-library, 90-digit outward-rounded interval certificate at the single preselected point z=1/2 gives det Sigma_sigma in [1.372485374000321,1.372485374000322] and lambda_min(Sigma_sigma)>0.037; it certifies no interval, critical value, finite-record statistic, or coverage. The scripts, stored reports, commands, environments, and SHA-256 manifests are listed in the archive. These numerical batteries and regression checks are sanity checks only. They never substitute for proof, and in particular cannot prove the stopped-prefix rate, compact-uniform calibration, infinite-dimensional tangent closure, moving-dimensional Gaussian coupling, necessity boundary, LAN remainder, or any other asymptotic/uniform statement.
+
+Your previous verdict on the earlier version was adverse and must remain part of the assessment. You recommended rejection, with possible resubmission only after substantial reconstruction, priority re-audit, and completion of key proofs. You said that you had found no counterexample destroying the renewal law, three-coordinate quotient, or two-state fibre arc, and judged those core mechanisms probably correct; but you found the manuscript below its claimed completeness and priority standard. Specifically, you regarded the O_P(N^{-1/3}) stopped separated-batch rate, compact-uniform image test, uniform complete-law calibration, stationary-record likelihood reductions, the Helmert “necessary and sufficient” coupling boundary, and the singular experiment as incompletely proved; you also identified inconsistent theorem numbering and dependency claims. You said the Helmert iff theorem was the weakest major technical point, the “complete singular experiment” wording exceeded the proved symmetric path, and the projection example was standard and too prominent. On priority you found the Bickel-Kwon omission material, required an explicit reduction against Ramirez-Cobo-Lillo/Meszaros-Telek, and required He-Zhang/generalized-Erlang comparisons. On scope you judged the paper primarily applied probability, realization theory, and asymptotic statistics, with the sampled-counter device serving as a model interpretation rather than an analysed or validated physical system; even after mathematical repair you did not regard the then-current architecture as a natural Journal of Physics Communications paper. You described it as three heterogeneous papers joined together and named Methodology and Computing in Applied Probability, or a reduced Stochastic Models paper, as more natural landing places. Your formal recommendation was Reject rather than major revision. The current PDF has responded by fixing the A-E registry, narrowing the stationary-record and projection claims, adding the missing priority comparisons, making the latched physical protocol explicit, and greatly expanding several proofs; we are not asking you to assume those repairs succeeded. Please test them when judging reachability.
+
+Now answer the following, in this order.
+
+1. Name the single most valuable NEW RESULT that would move this paper up a tier. Be specific: write an actual theorem statement with hypotheses and conclusion, not a topic such as “generalize the model,” “add applications,” or “strengthen identifiability.” If more than one result is genuinely competitive, rank them, but still identify your first choice.
+
+2. For each candidate, is it REACHABLE FROM THE MACHINERY ALREADY IN THIS PAPER? Identify exactly which existing lemmas, constructions, formulas, tangent spaces, likelihood expansions, similarity arguments, or estimates would feed the proof. Then identify the genuinely new ingredient that is missing. If it is not reachable from what we have, say so plainly and state what would be needed instead. Distinguish “a long but direct completion” from “a new idea of a different kind.”
+
+3. Rank the candidates by success probability multiplied by tier impact. Give the success probability as a number from 0% to 100%, not as a word, and separately score tier impact so that we can see how you formed the ranking. Treat “success” as obtaining a referee-grade proof without quietly adding hypotheses that drain the theorem of significance.
+
+4. Which of the following standard tier-raising levers actually applies here, and exactly how? For every lever you select, name the target theorem or literature benchmark; for every lever you reject, say why it is unavailable.
+
+   (a) settling a named open problem in the literature;
+   (b) proving something about the FIELD'S OWN standard objects rather than about objects this paper defines;
+   (c) removing a hypothesis from an existing theorem, especially one the paper currently assumes for convenience;
+   (d) a sharpness or matching-bound result showing an existing estimate cannot be improved.
+
+5. Even if we add your top-ranked result and prove it correctly, what is the strongest objection a referee at the higher tier would still raise against the paper? State the objection in its hardest credible form, and state exactly what evidence, theorem, reorganization, or deletion would resolve it.
+
+Constraints on your answer:
+
+- Do NOT answer the tier question by recommending a lower-tier journal. We already know where this lands as it stands. The question is what mathematical result would move it up.
+
+- Do NOT propose merely adding more theorems of the kind already present. An earlier round of ours mechanically accumulated results and moved nothing; six of our seven papers were then found to have priority omissions, because formally new statements composed from standard equivalences carry little independent priority. A longer list of model-specific corollaries, delta methods, tests, expansions, or reformulations is not a tier-raising answer.
+
+- If the honest conclusion is that no reachable result exists and this paper cannot rise, say that plainly. Identify the structural feature that caps it: for example, confinement to a bespoke killed-reset subclass, reduction to standard minimal-realization equivalence, lack of a field-standard object, or the need for a genuinely different physical/statistical problem. A clear, well-argued ceiling will be acted on; vague encouragement will not.
+
+A hard, specific negative is more useful to us than a soft positive. Every objection must come with the condition that would resolve it.

+Please read main.pdf as the current manuscript, “Renewal laws, quotient inversion, and Markovian fibres for a killed-reset D-MAP,” and give us a tier-raising mathematical assessment. This is not another journal-fit question. We already know where the paper lands as it stands. We want a named, concrete, reachable theorem target whose successful addition would move the paper up a tier.
+
+Here is the model and the present theorem package, stated so that the target you propose can be judged against the machinery actually available.
+
+The standing hypothesis is a stationary two-state D-MAP with hidden states R and D, positive rates Gamma and kappa_r, and sampling interval Delta tau > 0. Put
+
+  p = exp(-Gamma Delta tau),   s = exp(-kappa_r Delta tau),
+
+  b = integral_0^{Delta tau} kappa_r exp(-kappa_r r) exp[-Gamma(Delta tau-r)] dr,
+
+  a = integral_0^{Delta tau} kappa_r exp(-kappa_r r){1-exp[-Gamma(Delta tau-r)]} dr,
+
+and
+
+  T_0 = [[p,0],[b,s]],   T_1 = [[0,1-p],[0,a]].
+
+The convention is that (T_i)_{XY}=P(A_n=i,H_{n+1}=Y | H_n=X). The structural hypothesis T_1(R,R)=T_1(D,R)=0 means that every click leaves the counter latched in D at the sampling boundary. The paper derives these kernels from that explicit latched protocol, but does not claim that they describe generic D-MAP(2), generic dead-time detectors, or a detector allowing post-click same-bin recovery. If either killed entry is positive, a click need not regenerate at D and the renewal, quotient, and retained-cycle arguments do not follow.
+
+The main results are as follows.
+
+1. Record law (Theorem A). Under the standing hypothesis, the stationary binary output is a full-support renewal process. If G is the Palm number of zeros between consecutive clicks, rho=P(A_0=1), and lambda_hid=p-b, then
+
+  g_k=P(G=k)=e_D^T T_0^k T_1 1,   k>=0.
+
+For Gamma != kappa_r,
+
+  g_k = [kappa_r(1-p)p^k-Gamma(1-s)s^k]/(kappa_r-Gamma),
+  S_k=P(G>=k)=[kappa_r p^k-Gamma s^k]/(kappa_r-Gamma).
+
+On the diagonal Gamma=kappa_r, writing x=Gamma Delta tau and p=e^{-x},
+
+  g_k=p^k{(1-p)(1+xk)-xp},   S_k=p^k(1+xk).
+
+The hazard g_k/S_k is strictly increasing. For n>=1,
+
+  Cov(A_0,A_n)=rho(a-rho)lambda_hid^{n-1},
+
+with rho(a-rho)<0. The record is exactly 1-dependent iff lambda_hid=0, is not 0-dependent, and is never a finite-order Markov chain for positive parameters. In dimensionless coordinates x=Gamma Delta tau and y=kappa_r Delta tau,
+
+  lambda_hid=[y e^{-y}-x e^{-x}]/(y-x)
+
+off the diagonal, with derivative value e^{-x}(1-x) on it; its sharp global range is [-e^{-2},1), with the lower endpoint only at x=y=2. The paper also proves, for fixed positive rates as Delta tau=Delta tends to zero,
+
+  Delta E(G+1)=1/Gamma+1/kappa_r+Delta/2
+    +Gamma kappa_r(Gamma+kappa_r)Delta^4/720+O(Delta^6),
+
+and the corresponding first-order downward sampling bias in clicks per unit time.
+
+2. Three-inclusion quotient and physical image (Theorem B and its image propositions). Let r_0=P(A_0=1), r_1=P(A_0=1,A_1=1), and r_2=P(A_0=1,A_2=1). On r_0>0 and Delta_inv=r_1-r_0^2 != 0, define
+
+  a(r)=r_1/r_0,
+  lambda(r)=(r_2-r_0^2)/(r_1-r_0^2),
+
+  Phi(r)=(1-a(r)+lambda(r),
+          r_0[1-lambda(r)]-a(r)+lambda(r)).
+
+For the constrained model, Phi(r)=(p+s,ps). Thus three visible inclusion probabilities determine the unordered pair {p,s}, and, if Delta tau is known, the unordered rate set {Gamma,kappa_r}; they do not determine the physical labels. The exact image is also characterized. If p,s in (0,1) are the roots of z^2-Phi_1(r)z+Phi_2(r)=0, put
+
+  C(p,s)=(s log p-p log s)/(log p-log s) for p!=s,
+  C(p,p)=p(1-log p),
+  E(r)=r_1/r_0-1+C(p,s).
+
+Then r is generated by positive sampled-counter parameters iff E(r)=0. In a neighbourhood of every compact dimensionless parameter rectangle [epsilon,M]^2, this has a root-free real-analytic continuation e(r), and the positive image is exactly
+
+  e(r)=0,   D(r)=Phi_1(r)^2-4Phi_2(r)>=0,
+
+with rank D(e,D)=2 even on the exchange diagonal. On that compact chart r_0 and |Delta_inv| have explicit uniform positive lower bounds; away from |p-s|=0 the sorted-root and log-rate maps are uniformly stable.
+
+3. Markovian fibres. For the physical two-state K=T_0, q_*=Gamma/kappa_r, and I_*=[min(1,q_*),max(1,q_*)], the complete fibre among two-state substochastic killed-reset kernels with the same visible law is the injective closed arc
+
+  K(q) = [[p-b+bq, b(1-q)(q-q_*)/q],
+          [bq,       s+b(1-q)]],    q in I_*.
+
+Its endpoints are the two triangular kernels obtained by exchanging Gamma and kappa_r. Off the exchange diagonal its interior kernels and killing deficits are strictly positive and the physical triangular family meets it only at the endpoints; on the diagonal the whole fibre collapses to a singleton. For a pure serial n-phase kernel exp(Delta tau Q(theta)), with arbitrary positive rates and repetitions allowed, the first 2n Palm-tail coordinates S_0,...,S_{2n-1} recover the minimal confluent-Prony polynomial prod_j(z-exp[-theta_j Delta tau]), including multiplicities, hence the unordered rate multiset. For an unrestricted minimal interior n-state killed-reset kernel K>0 with (I-K)1>0, reset-preserving similarities M1=1 and e_n^T M=e_n^T give a local positivity-preserving visible-fibre manifold of exact dimension (n-1)^2, modulo the finite reset-preserving permutation group.
+
+4. Fixed-setting inference (Theorems C-E). Retaining only cycles fully observed in a stationary length-N record, define the three-coordinate complete-cycle estimator and Palm rewards
+
+  U_j=(1, 1{G_j=0}, 1{G_j=1}+1{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0})^T,
+  L_j=G_j+1,   Y_j=U_j-r L_j.
+
+Then Y_j is centred and 1-dependent and
+
+  sqrt(N)(rhat_N-r) = N^{-1/2} sum_{j=0}^{K_N-1}Y_j+o_P(1)
+                    => N(0,Sigma_r),
+
+  Sigma_r=mu^{-1}{Var(Y_0)+Cov(Y_0,Y_1)+Cov(Y_1,Y_0)}.
+
+With b_N=floor(N^{1/3}), plug-in centring, and a prespecified gate M_N>=m_N where m_N tends to infinity and m_N=o(N/b_N), the separated-batch covariance estimator has operator-norm error O_P(N^{-1/3}) on an event of probability tending to one. Delta transport through Phi gives the Gaussian limit for (p+s,ps); if (p-s)^2>0, the manuscript gives the explicit sorted-root Jacobian and, for known Delta tau, the log-rate CLT. No root CLT is claimed on the exchange diagonal.
+
+Theorem D combines (i) a truncated distributional distance from the compact null family, using all binary words up to L_N=max{3,floor[(log_2 N)/8]}, and (ii) a root-free adjacent-gap score. Its null rejection probability converges uniformly to alpha on [epsilon,M]^2, including N^{-1/4} root splittings. It is consistent against any fixed stationary ergodic binary law at positive distributional distance from the null, uniformly for geometrically mixing, uniformly separated classes. At an exchange point it supplies the explicit fixed-marginal Markov-gap alternative
+
+  Q_eta(l|k)=g_l{1+eta h(k)h(l)},
+  h(k)=1{k=1}-(g_1/g_2)1{k=2},
+
+which preserves r_0,r_1,r_2 exactly but changes P(10101) by the factor 1+eta. Along eta_N=t/sqrt(N), the stationary-record likelihood is LAN with information I=(E_g h(G)^2)^2/mu, and the score attains the Gaussian-shift power envelope for this one-dimensional subexperiment.
+
+There is also a compact-uniform physical-image test based on e(r)=0 and D(r)>=0. After residualization its statistic is Z_e^2+(Z_d^-)^2. At separated roots its limit is chi-square_1; on the exchange diagonal it is (chi-square_1+chi-square_2)/2 in the chi-bar-square sense. A log-N selector has compact-uniform asymptotic size at most alpha over [epsilon,M]^2, including intermediate sequences where the selector need not settle, and the test is consistent against the stated full-support renewal alternatives with e(r)!=0 or D(r)<0 and positive-definite constraint covariance.
+
+5. Supplementary claims. At an interior exchange point, the manuscript starts from the Bickel-Kwon fixed-marginal Markov interaction space and proves that imposing preservation of r_0,r_1,r_2 removes exactly the atom direction q(0,0):
+
+  T={q in L^2(g x g): E(q|G_0)=E(q|G_1)=0, q(0,0)=0}.
+
+It constructs positive two-sided paths preserving g and all three inclusions exactly, obtains calendar-time information mu^{-1}Pi_T, fixed-dimensional retained-record LAN subject to an explicit endpoint condition, and a weighted infinite score test with pointwise power against every nonzero fixed direction and exchange-local null calibration.

Finally, for a deterministic product experiment of m_N(z)=floor(N/mu_z) independent complete Palm gaps and a symmetric split p=z+hN^{-1/4}, s=z-hN^{-1/4}, uniformly for z in a compact subset of (0,1) and bounded h,
+
+  log(dP_{N,z,h}/dP_{N,z,0})
+    =h^2 Delta_{N,z}-(h^4/2)I(z)+o_P(1),
+  Delta_{N,z}=>N(0,I(z)).
+
+This identifies h^2, not its sign, and is expressly not a stationary-record endpoint equivalence or an exhaustion of all local directions. The pointwise projection result at z=1/2 is only a standard metric-projection example, not a test or confidence procedure.

Specifically, you regarded the O_P(N^{-1/3}) stopped separated-batch rate, compact-uniform image test, uniform complete-law calibration, stationary-record likelihood reductions, the Helmert “necessary and sufficient” coupling boundary, and the singular experiment as incompletely proved; you also identified inconsistent theorem numbering and dependency claims. You said the Helmert iff theorem was the weakest major technical point, the “complete singular experiment” wording exceeded the proved symmetric path, and the projection example was standard and too prominent. On priority you found the Bickel-Kwon omission material, required an explicit reduction against Ramirez-Cobo-Lillo/Meszaros-Telek, and required He-Zhang/generalized-Erlang comparisons. On scope you judged the paper primarily applied probability, realization theory, and asymptotic statistics, with the sampled-counter device serving as a model interpretation rather than an analysed or validated physical system; even after mathematical repair you did not regard the then-current architecture as a natural Journal of Physics Communications paper. You described it as three heterogeneous papers joined together and named Methodology and Computing in Applied Probability, or a reduced Stochastic Models paper, as more natural landing places. Your formal recommendation was Reject rather than major revision. The current PDF has responded by fixing the A-E registry, narrowing the stationary-record and projection claims, adding the missing priority comparisons, making the latched physical protocol explicit, and greatly expanding several proofs; we are not asking you to assume those repairs succeeded. Please test them when judging reachability.
+
+Now answer the following, in this order.
+
+1. Name the single most valuable NEW RESULT that would move this paper up a tier. Be specific: write an actual theorem statement with hypotheses and conclusion, not a topic such as “generalize the model,” “add applications,” or “strengthen identifiability.” If more than one result is genuinely competitive, rank them, but still identify your first choice.
+
+2. For each candidate, is it REACHABLE FROM THE MACHINERY ALREADY IN THIS PAPER? Identify exactly which existing lemmas, constructions, formulas, tangent spaces, likelihood expansions, similarity arguments, or estimates would feed the proof. Then identify the genuinely new ingredient that is missing. If it is not reachable from what we have, say so plainly and state what would be needed instead. Distinguish “a long but direct completion” from “a new idea of a different kind.”
+
+3. Rank the candidates by success probability multiplied by tier impact. Give the success probability as a number from 0% to 100%, not as a word, and separately score tier impact so that we can see how you formed the ranking. Treat “success” as obtaining a referee-grade proof without quietly adding hypotheses that drain the theorem of significance.
+
+4. Which of the following standard tier-raising levers actually applies here, and exactly how? For every lever you select, name the target theorem or literature benchmark; for every lever you reject, say why it is unavailable.
+
+   (a) settling a named open problem in the literature;
+   (b) proving something about the FIELD'S OWN standard objects rather than about objects this paper defines;
+   (c) removing a hypothesis from an existing theorem, especially one the paper currently assumes for convenience;
+   (d) a sharpness or matching-bound result showing an existing estimate cannot be improved.
+
+5. Even if we add your top-ranked result and prove it correctly, what is the strongest objection a referee at the higher tier would still raise against the paper? State the objection in its hardest credible form, and state exactly what evidence, theorem, reorganization, or deletion would resolve it.
+
+Constraints on your answer:
+
+- Do NOT answer the tier question by recommending a lower-tier journal. We already know where this lands as it stands. The question is what mathematical result would move it up.
+
+- Do NOT propose merely adding more theorems of the kind already present. An earlier round of ours mechanically accumulated results and moved nothing; six of our seven papers were then found to have priority omissions, because formally new statements composed from standard equivalences carry little independent priority. A longer list of model-specific corollaries, delta methods, tests, expansions, or reformulations is not a tier-raising answer.
+
+- If the honest conclusion is that no reachable result exists and this paper cannot rise, say that plainly. Identify the structural feature that caps it: for example, confinement to a bespoke killed-reset subclass, reduction to standard minimal-realization equivalence, lack of a field-standard object, or the need for a genuinely different physical/statistical problem. A clear, well-argued ceiling will be acted on; vague encouragement will not.
+
+A hard, specific negative is more useful to us than a soft positive. Every objection must come with the condition that would resolve it.

+Please read main.pdf as the current manuscript, “Renewal laws, quotient inversion, and Markovian fibres for a killed-reset D-MAP,” and give us a tier-raising mathematical assessment. This is not another journal-fit question. We already know where the paper lands as it stands. We want a named, concrete, reachable theorem target whose successful addition would move the paper up a tier.
+
+Here is the model and the present theorem package, stated so that the target you propose can be judged against the machinery actually available.
+
+The standing hypothesis is a stationary two-state D-MAP with hidden states R and D, positive rates Gamma and kappa_r, and sampling interval Delta tau > 0. Put
+
+  p = exp(-Gamma Delta tau),   s = exp(-kappa_r Delta tau),
+
+  b = integral_0^{Delta tau} kappa_r exp(-kappa_r r) exp[-Gamma(Delta tau-r)] dr,
+
+  a = integral_0^{Delta tau} kappa_r exp(-kappa_r r){1-exp[-Gamma(Delta tau-r)]} dr,
+
+and
+
+  T_0 = [[p,0],[b,s]],   T_1 = [[0,1-p],[0,a]].
+
+The convention is that (T_i)_{XY}=P(A_n=i,H_{n+1}=Y | H_n=X). The structural hypothesis T_1(R,R)=T_1(D,R)=0 means that every click leaves the counter latched in D at the sampling boundary. The paper derives these kernels from that explicit latched protocol, but does not claim that they describe generic D-MAP(2), generic dead-time detectors, or a detector allowing post-click same-bin recovery. If either killed entry is positive, a click need not regenerate at D and the renewal, quotient, and retained-cycle arguments do not follow.
+
+The main results are as follows.
+
+1. Record law (Theorem A). Under the standing hypothesis, the stationary binary output is a full-support renewal process. If G is the Palm number of zeros between consecutive clicks, rho=P(A_0=1), and lambda_hid=p-b, then
+
+  g_k=P(G=k)=e_D^T T_0^k T_1 1,   k>=0.
+
+For Gamma != kappa_r,
+
+  g_k = [kappa_r(1-p)p^k-Gamma(1-s)s^k]/(kappa_r-Gamma),
+  S_k=P(G>=k)=[kappa_r p^k-Gamma s^k]/(kappa_r-Gamma).
+
+On the diagonal Gamma=kappa_r, writing x=Gamma Delta tau and p=e^{-x},
+
+  g_k=p^k{(1-p)(1+xk)-xp},   S_k=p^k(1+xk).
+
+The hazard g_k/S_k is strictly increasing. For n>=1,
+
+  Cov(A_0,A_n)=rho(a-rho)lambda_hid^{n-1},
+
+with rho(a-rho)<0. The record is exactly 1-dependent iff lambda_hid=0, is not 0-dependent, and is never a finite-order Markov chain for positive parameters. In dimensionless coordinates x=Gamma Delta tau and y=kappa_r Delta tau,
+
+  lambda_hid=[y e^{-y}-x e^{-x}]/(y-x)
+
+off the diagonal, with derivative value e^{-x}(1-x) on it; its sharp global range is [-e^{-2},1), with the lower endpoint only at x=y=2. The paper also proves, for fixed positive rates as Delta tau=Delta tends to zero,
+
+  Delta E(G+1)=1/Gamma+1/kappa_r+Delta/2
+    +Gamma kappa_r(Gamma+kappa_r)Delta^4/720+O(Delta^6),
+
+and the corresponding first-order downward sampling bias in clicks per unit time.
+
+2. Three-inclusion quotient and physical image (Theorem B and its image propositions). Let r_0=P(A_0=1), r_1=P(A_0=1,A_1=1), and r_2=P(A_0=1,A_2=1). On r_0>0 and Delta_inv=r_1-r_0^2 != 0, define
+
+  a(r)=r_1/r_0,
+  lambda(r)=(r_2-r_0^2)/(r_1-r_0^2),
+
+  Phi(r)=(1-a(r)+lambda(r),
+          r_0[1-lambda(r)]-a(r)+lambda(r)).
+
+For the constrained model, Phi(r)=(p+s,ps). Thus three visible inclusion probabilities determine the unordered pair {p,s}, and, if Delta tau is known, the unordered rate set {Gamma,kappa_r}; they do not determine the physical labels. The exact image is also characterized. If p,s in (0,1) are the roots of z^2-Phi_1(r)z+Phi_2(r)=0, put
+
+  C(p,s)=(s log p-p log s)/(log p-log s) for p!=s,
+  C(p,p)=p(1-log p),
+  E(r)=r_1/r_0-1+C(p,s).
+
+Then r is generated by positive sampled-counter parameters iff E(r)=0. In a neighbourhood of every compact dimensionless parameter rectangle [epsilon,M]^2, this has a root-free real-analytic continuation e(r), and the positive image is exactly
+
+  e(r)=0,   D(r)=Phi_1(r)^2-4Phi_2(r)>=0,
+
+with rank D(e,D)=2 even on the exchange diagonal. On that compact chart r_0 and |Delta_inv| have explicit uniform positive lower bounds; away from |p-s|=0 the sorted-root and log-rate maps are uniformly stable.
+
+3. Markovian fibres. For the physical two-state K=T_0, q_*=Gamma/kappa_r, and I_*=[min(1,q_*),max(1,q_*)], the complete fibre among two-state substochastic killed-reset kernels with the same visible law is the injective closed arc
+
+  K(q) = [[p-b+bq, b(1-q)(q-q_*)/q],
+          [bq,       s+b(1-q)]],    q in I_*.
+
+Its endpoints are the two triangular kernels obtained by exchanging Gamma and kappa_r. Off the exchange diagonal its interior kernels and killing deficits are strictly positive and the physical triangular family meets it only at the endpoints; on the diagonal the whole fibre collapses to a singleton. For a pure serial n-phase kernel exp(Delta tau Q(theta)), with arbitrary positive rates and repetitions allowed, the first 2n Palm-tail coordinates S_0,...,S_{2n-1} recover the minimal confluent-Prony polynomial prod_j(z-exp[-theta_j Delta tau]), including multiplicities, hence the unordered rate multiset. For an unrestricted minimal interior n-state killed-reset kernel K>0 with (I-K)1>0, reset-preserving similarities M1=1 and e_n^T M=e_n^T give a local positivity-preserving visible-fibre manifold of exact dimension (n-1)^2, modulo the finite reset-preserving permutation group.
+
+4. Fixed-setting inference (Theorems C-E). Retaining only cycles fully observed in a stationary length-N record, define the three-coordinate complete-cycle estimator and Palm rewards
+
+  U_j=(1, 1{G_j=0}, 1{G_j=1}+1{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0})^T,
+  L_j=G_j+1,   Y_j=U_j-r L_j.
+
+Then Y_j is centred and 1-dependent and
+
+  sqrt(N)(rhat_N-r) = N^{-1/2} sum_{j=0}^{K_N-1}Y_j+o_P(1)
+                    => N(0,Sigma_r),
+
+  Sigma_r=mu^{-1}{Var(Y_0)+Cov(Y_0,Y_1)+Cov(Y_1,Y_0)}.
+
+With b_N=floor(N^{1/3}), plug-in centring, and a prespecified gate M_N>=m_N where m_N tends to infinity and m_N=o(N/b_N), the separated-batch covariance estimator has operator-norm error O_P(N^{-1/3}) on an event of probability tending to one. Delta transport through Phi gives the Gaussian limit for (p+s,ps); if (p-s)^2>0, the manuscript gives the explicit sorted-root Jacobian and, for known Delta tau, the log-rate CLT. No root CLT is claimed on the exchange diagonal.
+
+Theorem D combines (i) a truncated distributional distance from the compact null family, using all binary words up to L_N=max{3,floor[(log_2 N)/8]}, and (ii) a root-free adjacent-gap score. Its null rejection probability converges uniformly to alpha on [epsilon,M]^2, including N^{-1/4} root splittings. It is consistent against any fixed stationary ergodic binary law at positive distributional distance from the null, uniformly for geometrically mixing, uniformly separated classes. At an exchange point it supplies the explicit fixed-marginal Markov-gap alternative
+
+  Q_eta(l|k)=g_l{1+eta h(k)h(l)},
+  h(k)=1{k=1}-(g_1/g_2)1{k=2},
+
+which preserves r_0,r_1,r_2 exactly but changes P(10101) by the factor 1+eta. Along eta_N=t/sqrt(N), the stationary-record likelihood is LAN with information I=(E_g h(G)^2)^2/mu, and the score attains the Gaussian-shift power envelope for this one-dimensional subexperiment.
+
+There is also a compact-uniform physical-image test based on e(r)=0 and D(r)>=0. After residualization its statistic is Z_e^2+(Z_d^-)^2. At separated roots its limit is chi-square_1; on the exchange diagonal it is (chi-square_1+chi-square_2)/2 in the chi-bar-square sense. A log-N selector has compact-uniform asymptotic size at most alpha over [epsilon,M]^2, including intermediate sequences where the selector need not settle, and the test is consistent against the stated full-support renewal alternatives with e(r)!=0 or D(r)<0 and positive-definite constraint covariance.
+
+5. Supplementary claims. At an interior exchange point, the manuscript starts from the Bickel-Kwon fixed-marginal Markov interaction space and proves that imposing preservation of r_0,r_1,r_2 removes exactly the atom direction q(0,0):
+
+  T={q in L^2(g x g): E(q|G_0)=E(q|G_1)=0, q(0,0)=0}.
+
+It constructs positive two-sided paths preserving g and all three inclusions exactly, obtains calendar-time information mu^{-1}Pi_T, fixed-dimensional retained-record LAN subject to an explicit endpoint condition, and a weighted infinite score test with pointwise power against every nonzero fixed direction and exchange-local null calibration.
+
+For the equal-rate tail S_j=p_0^j(1+x_0 j), its moving Helmert layer of dimension d_J=(J+1)^2-1 has a uniform Euclidean coupling to N(0,I_{d_J}) over N^{-1/2}-local physical nulls iff
+
+  n_N S_{J_N}^2 -> infinity,   n_N=N/mu.
+
+In the second-order band this is equivalent to
+
+  2x_0 J_N <= log n_N+2 log log n_N-omega_N
+
+for some omega_N->infinity; at a fixed critical correction the corner instead has a compound-Poisson obstruction. Finally, for a deterministic product experiment of m_N(z)=floor(N/mu_z) independent complete Palm gaps and a symmetric split p=z+hN^{-1/4}, s=z-hN^{-1/4}, uniformly for z in a compact subset of (0,1) and bounded h,
+
+  log(dP_{N,z,h}/dP_{N,z,0})
+    =h^2 Delta_{N,z}-(h^4/2)I(z)+o_P(1),
+  Delta_{N,z}=>N(0,I(z)).
+
+This identifies h^2, not its sign, and is expressly not a stationary-record endpoint equivalence or an exhaustion of all local directions. The pointwise projection result at z=1/2 is only a standard metric-projection example, not a test or confidence procedure.
+
+Imported results versus our residual claims must be kept separate. The D-MAP/MAP framework is due to Neuts, Lucantoni, and Alfa-Neuts. Zamparo supplies renewal-binary finite-law results and the finite-order Markov criterion. Geometric MAP2 covariance behaviour was already studied by Ramirez-Cobo and Carrizosa. Kalman minimal-realization uniqueness and its MAP formulations are standard; Ramirez-Cobo, Wiper, and Lillo established MAP2 nonidentifiability, and Ramirez-Cobo and Lillo gave a complete weak-equivalence classification for MAP2/MAP3. Meszaros and Telek gave canonical discrete MAP2/RAP2 coordinates. Generalized-Erlang, acyclic phase-type, Coxian minimality, repeated-pole, and representation theory come from Cumani, O'Cinneide, He-Zhang, and related literature; confluent Prony recovery is standard. Regenerative CLTs, batch means, the multivariate delta method, stationary-ergodic distributional-distance testing, chi-bar-square boundary theory, generic LAN, and metric-projection limits are imported. Bickel-Kwon supply the doubly centred known-marginal Markov tangent and additive projection. The Gaussian-approximation proof imports Meckes's Stein estimate and the Bolbotowski-Bouchitte Z_2/W_2 comparison. The N^{-1/4} loss-of-identifiability mechanism has general antecedents in locally conic and loss-of-identifiability theory.
+
+What we can honestly claim as ours is therefore narrower: the model-specific closed Palm law, hazard and exact dependence threshold; the sharp hidden-mode range and sampling expansion; the unusually low-order three-inclusion quotient formula and analytic physical image; the exact intersection of the weak-equivalence orbit with this killed-reset Markov cone; the first-2n sampled-tail formulation for the serial subclass; the reset-preserving subgroup dimension/positivity calculation; the complete-cycle bookkeeping and model-specific tests; the additional atom constraint, positive realizing paths and calendar scaling in the Markov-gap calculation; the particular equal-rate moving-layer boundary if its proof is correct; and the explicit complete-Palm-gap symmetric-split likelihood. Even the three-inclusion formula is still described in the paper only as a candidate original result pending a complete reduction against Meszaros-Telek canonical DMAP2 coordinates. If the fibre arc drops out immediately from Ramirez-Cobo-Lillo after imposing the reset constraints, it is only an explicit cone-intersection corollary. The serial identifiability statement is only a finite-coordinate sampled specialization of generalized-Erlang/Coxian theory. The (n-1)^2 result is a short Lie-orbit consequence, not a new general realization theorem.
+
+The priority position must be assessed against us, not in our favour: the earlier external review found that the previous version omitted or underweighted at least three direct antecedent groups that materially shrink the novelty boundary: Bickel-Kwon for the fixed-marginal Markov tangent, Ramirez-Cobo-Lillo and Meszaros-Telek for weak equivalence/canonical order-two representations, and He-Zhang and the generalized-Erlang/Coxian literature for serial identifiability. The current manuscript has added these comparisons and reduced its claims, but it has not proved that the three-inclusion quotient is absent from canonical DMAP2 coordinates.
+
+Machine-verification evidence has a deliberately limited role. The reproducibility archive records Python 3.10.11, NumPy 1.26.4, SciPy 1.12.0, and mpmath 1.3.0; its unit suite reports 53 tests, all passing. The finite regression scripts check the image equation, hidden-mode bound, small-Delta expansion, Markov-gap centring and inclusion preservation, length-five separation, finite tangent bases, Helmert orthogonality and covariance identities, selected coupling error terms, the singular-gap expansion, exact two-state fibre endpoints, serial n=2,3,4 Hankel recovery including collisions, and visible-law invariance under explicit reset-preserving similarities. Stored examples report image residuals around 10^{-16}, two-state survival-coordinate errors around 10^{-16}, and exact recovery of the tested unordered rate multisets. A standard-library, 90-digit outward-rounded interval certificate at the single preselected point z=1/2 gives det Sigma_sigma in [1.372485374000321,1.372485374000322] and lambda_min(Sigma_sigma)>0.037; it certifies no interval, critical value, finite-record statistic, or coverage. The scripts, stored reports, commands, environments, and SHA-256 manifests are listed in the archive. These numerical batteries and regression checks are sanity checks only. They never substitute for proof, and in particular cannot prove the stopped-prefix rate, compact-uniform calibration, infinite-dimensional tangent closure, moving-dimensional Gaussian coupling, necessity boundary, LAN remainder, or any other asymptotic/uniform statement.
+
